Gonsciousness i n the W e s t . Srila Prabhupada had numerous ideas and desires on h o w
to develop this community to be a replica of V r i n d a b a n i n I n d i a . H a v i n g                       left
V r i n d a b a n in 1965 to spread the glories of L o r d K r s n a , Srila Prabhupada n o w set

Bhaktivedánta         Vidyápitha is a two-year f u l l time study course for the resident
devotees      at G o v a r d h a n F c o V i l l a g e . I t covers the systematic study of S r i m a d -
Bhágavatam        and S r i C a i t a n y a Caritámrta           translated       and commented                  by     Srila
Prabhupáda. I n addition, commentaries by ácáryas like Srila Visvanátha C a k r a v a r t i
Thákura are also referred to enrich the understanding of the students. T h e course is
based on a ' 5 S ' model as described below and helps to provide a wholesome, all
round development of a sincere practitioner of B h a k t i , as per its vision and mission
statements.
Bhágavata Subodhiní                                                                                  t'J'
VISION
Inspiring     and inculcating Vaisnava qualities and a mood of selfless                 service
in the followers     of the International    Society for Krsna Gonsciousness,             by
creating a strong sástric foundation        for their spiritual    lives.
MISSION
Assisting the devotees in understanding             the glorious contribution      of Srila
Prabhupada's      books, by systematically     studying them and            contemplating
practical     ways for application     of their teachings through an           effective
educational framework        as envisaged by the Gaudiya               Vaisnava
parampará.
The Svádhyáya aspect, involves five elements -
a.   Sravanam (Hearing) - Daily classes are conducted progressing verse by verse
along with discussions on the purports. Special seminars covering important
topics for character development such as leadership, Vaisnava dealings, etc.
are organized once a month.
b. Mananam (RecoUection) - Daily closed book tests are conducted based on
the previous day's coverage of the syllabus to ensure the students' regular
study. In addition, a weekly review is organized to recap the week's study.
c.   Nididhyásanam (Contemplation) - Vidyápitha studies also include a channel
to express one's gathered learning in the form of open book essays and
assignments. Students are required to sift through the Canto looking for
common themes, or elaborating on a notable phrase hased on what they have
studied and learnt.
d. Pathanam (Recitation) - No Vedic institution is complete without recitation
of scriptural verses. At Bhaktivedanta Vidyápitha, students daily recite select
verses from the on-going section of the Bhágavatam.
e.   Kírtanam (Speaking) - The students are also trained in communication skills
and impromptu speech and thought, by asking them to present on various
topics or themes at regular intervals.
Sádhana involves the standard spiritual practices in I S K C O N such as the morning
program. On the other hand, Sevá consists of voluntary work in the farm, such as
serving cows or facilitating guests. Students are encouraged to study in groups, work
in teams for projects and this strengthens the Sahga element. The Sadácara aspect
involves the overall practice of the other four elements, grooming the student's
interpersonal skills, and encouraging one's dealings based on respect, gratitude and
interdependance for mutual growth and sustenance.
Bhaktivedánta Vidyápitha has been a dream come true for many devotees who desire
to study the Bhaktivedánta Purports. In sight of this, an online Bhágavatam course
{vidyapitha.in)   had been made available, where students can study at their
convenience.

A B O U T BHAGAVATA SUBODHINÍ
Srimad-Bhagavatam has earned its novelty amongst the modern philosophers,
theologians and historians alike due to the unprecedented efforts of His Divine Grace
A . C . Bhaktivedánta Swámi Prabhupáda, the Founder Acharya of the International
Society of Krsna Consciousness ( I S K C O N ) . He personally undertook the herculean
task of translating and providing commentary in English - of this voluminous
masterpiece - which was originally rendered in the Sanskrit language. He had
unveiled to the English-speaking world the secrets of the Vedic Culture that focused
on perfecting the humán form of life. The elité scholars of the 70's were blessed with
such a voluminous presentation of the Srimad-Bhágavatam that they took to
scrutinizing its work and content. I n fact, many universities and colleges across the
United States had included this work in their academic libraries.
Having begun his society in the Bowery quarter of New York City, Bhaktivedánta
SwámI delivered daily classes on the Bhágavatam where most of his audiences were
the beatniks of the counter culture - fondly known as the hippies. They took to the
illuminating radiance of the Bhágavatam and while basking in its splendor; the
hippies hegan to turn a new leaf. Society witnessed their change in character,
appearance and behavior. "Who were once a thorn in the social body of New Yorkers,
the hippies, had now become fuU-fledged contributing members of the society.
Bhaktivedánta Swámi uncannily called them "the happies." This unique dual class
appreciation from extrémé ends of the social strata and an incredible effect on an
individual's persona is rarely observed with any normál book or literature; but the
Bhágavatam had that potency.
BHÁGAVATAM COMPARED TO LORD'S BODY
Srimad-Bhágavatam is a classic that is able to churn out endless life lessons,
inspiration, philosophical knowledge and more importantly the purpose of life. Its
LVé*7,m                         About Bhagavata Subodhini
glories are limitless and it presents the tatest knowledge about the identity, nature
and character of the Supreme Creator, the Supreme Lord Sri Krsna. The Srimad-
Bhágavatam     has twelve cantos,          which     represent        the different limbs of            the
transcendental body of the Supreme Lord.
pádau yadiyau prathama-dvitiyau                trtiya-türyau    kathitau     yad-ürü
nábhis tathá paitcama eva sastho            bhujántarath       dor-yugalath     tathányau

"The first and second cantos of the Bhágavatam are Sri Krsna's lotus feet. The
third and fourth cantos are His thighs. The fifth canto is His navel. The sixth
canto is His chest. The seventh and eighth cantos are His arms. The ninth
canto is His throat. The tenth canto is His beautiful lotus-face. The eleventh
canto is His forehead. The twelfth canto is His head. I bow down to that Lord,
the ocean of mercy whose color is like that of a tamála tree and who appears in
this world for the welfare of all. I worship Him as the bridge for crossing the
unfathomable ocean of matériái existence. The Bhágavatam has appeared as
His very Self." [Padnta Purdna]
SRÍLA PRABHUPADA'S EMPHASIS ON SYSTEMATIC STUDY
Srila Prabhupáda compared Bhágavatam study with taking darsan of the deity of
Lord Krsna, which is done gradually from Lord's lotus feet to the lotus face. He
emphasizes a step-by-step study of the Bhágavatam in his preface to Srimad-
Bhágavatam: "Srimad-Bhdgavatam             begins with the definition of the ultimate source.
It is a bona fide commentary             on the Veddnta-sütra            by the same author,        Srila
Vydsadeva, and gradually it develops into nine cantos up to the highest state of God
realization.   The   only     qualification    one    needs      to    study    this   great     book    of
transcendental knowledge is to proceed step by step cautiously and not jump                       forward
haphazardly as with an ordinary book. It should be gone through chapter by chapter,
one after another. The reading matter is so arranged with its originál Sanskrit text, its
English transliteration,     synonyms,     translation     and purports        so that one is sure to
become a God-realized soul at the end of finishing the first nine cantos."
Bhagavata Subodhiní
ÁCÁRYAS' UNPARALLELED CONTRIBUTION
The Vedic Encyclopedia, Srimad-Bhágavatam, like an ocean, is expansive with its
nominal and phenomenal subject matters and deep in its spiritual and philosophical
insights, making it a text for both research and relish by many. Srila Prabhupáda
mentions in his purport to the first verse of Bhágavatam: "...there is no doubt about
the authority of the Bhágavatam.   Within the past five hundred years, many erudite
scholars and dcdryas like Jiva Gosvdmi, Sandtana Gosvdmi, Visvandtha       Cakravarti,
Vallabhdcdrya,   and many other distinguished scholars even after the time of      Lord
Caitanya made elaborate commentaries on the Bhágavatam.      And the serious student
would do ivell to attempt to go through them to better relish the      transcendental
messages."
Most verses of the Bhágavatam are given descriptive commentaries by various
Gaudiya-vaisnava ácáryas. Amongst them is the esteemed           commentator       Srila
Visvanátha Cakravarti Thákura. His explanations are devotional, relishing, logical,
highly regarded in the study circles of Bhágavatam, and are often quoted in the
Bhaktivedánta purports. He is well known for giving connections between verses and
chapters, presenting the conversations and prayers in an interesting dialogue manner
and giving positive interpretations to the blasphemous words of demons directed
towards the Lord and His devotees. Unsurprisingly therefore, the various links
between verses, sections, chapters and even cantos, and additional information that
are presented in Bhágavata Subodhini, find their major source in the Sárártha darsini
commentary of Cakravarti Thákura.
BHAGAVATA SUBODHINÍ aims at helping the           readet to relish studying or reading
through the Srimad-Bhágavatam. Regular readers of the Bhágavatam might often
have the experience of - becoming lost in the multitudes of conversations and
flashbacks; challenging to keep track of the various personalities and episodes
mentioned; complicated technical aspects like creation, Universal form and time etc.
Because of this, readers tend to read selective sections or pastimes from the
Bhágavatam rather than endeavoring for a systematic approach. Having taken into
consideration all these academic anomalies that inhihits one from fully delighting in
the wonders of the Bhágavatam, the Bhaktivedánta Vidyápitha designed a systematic
framework to present the Bhágavatam in a lucid manner - Bhágavata     Subodhini.
About Bhagavata Subodhini
Bhágavata Subodhini simplifies the apparent complexity i n understanding the
Bhágavatam, by uncovering and integrating the explanations of Srila Prabhupáda
and the previous ácáryas, and thus caters to the taste and need of avid readers,
scholars, and even newcomers to the Srimad-Bhágavatam.
Therefore, anyone who hasn't even heard of the Bhágavatam can read through
Bhagavata Subodhini and develop an interest in the originál manuscript. However,
this book is not meant to be a replacement for a detailed study of Bhágavatam, hut a
complement for devotees, students and curious readers. It is therefore advised that
one simultaneously refer to the Bhágavatam while going through this book, and study
Bhágavatam with the assistance of Bhagavata Subodhini. We hope that this attempt
to sérve the Vaisnava community would be pleasing to all the Vaisnavas. The
following is a brief description of the various features of Bhágavata Subodhini.
OVERVIEW
This is the main section of Bhágavata Subodhini that gives an overview of the third
canto, while the other sections sérve as auxiliary contents that enhance one's
appreciation of the third canto. The following are the various features of this section.
•     Canto 3 A t a Glancé - Overview of the entire canto presented in a flowchart.
•    Chapter Overview chart - Provides a bird's eye view of the major sections and
subsections of a chapter.
•     Section - A group of verses that fali under the same heading.
•     Subsection - A sub group of verses under a section
•    Verse synopsis - A brief summary of a verse that highlights its import (a
condensed version of its translation by Srila Prabhupáda and Srila Visvanátha
Cakravarti thákura). A l l the verses are duly numbered and important keywords
of the Sanskrit verse are given in brackets.
•    L i n k - The connection between two verses or sections or subsections, which is
often taken from Srila Visvanátha Cakravarti tháküra's Sárártha Darsini
commentary.
•     Sanskrit Verse - A most vitai verse of the chapter presented in the flow, which is
worth memorizing.
•     Table - A list of items under a section
•     Llow C h a r t - Cives a bird's eye view of a sequense of events or the flow of
prayers.
•   Note - A vitai comment or explanation on a verse or a section, mainly related to
the flow of the chapter, taken from the purports of Sríla Prabhupáda and/or Sríla
Visvanátha Cakravarti Tháküra for enhancing the understanding of those verses
or sections, their context and philosophical depth. [However, when Sríla
Visvanátha Cakravarti Tháküra gives a unique and different perspective other
than the one given by Srila Prabhupáda, his name is mentioned in that Note.]
SPECIAL VERSES
This section contains select verses form a canto that carry deep and extraordinary
import in terms of philosophy (siddhanta) and emotions (bhava) that can increasingly
enhance the reader's appreciation. These verses can be considered as jewels that are
found in the depths of the ocean and the ácáryas, our guides, help us in accessing
them. We've provided a list of few citations by the various acaryas (wherever
possible), focusing on the numerous ways one can understand a single verse. These
different angles of vision in relishing the verses of the Bhágavatam are the glory of
the Vaisnava ácáryas for their astounding realization and expert presentation. The list
of citations are not exhaustive, and surely all verses of the Bhágavatam are equally
important, nevertheless, from these few pearls of Special Verses, one can only
imagine the pelagic treasures from the entire ocean of the Bhágavatam.
THEMATIC COMPILATIONS
This section consists of multiple prominent themes that are present in the specific
cantos of Srimad Bhágavatam. Each compilation contains a comprehensive list of
verses on a particular theme. The objective of this section is to make the readers
appreciate the cross sectional overview on a specific theme revealed in various
chapters of a canto, in addition to inspiring one to study the Bhágavatam from
various angles. These compilations are based on the analysis and research of many
devotees as a part of their study at the Bhaktivedánta Vidyápitha.
APPENDIX
The extensive lists of reference matériái from the Bhágavatam cantos are presented in
this section. Unlike the Thematic Compilations, these materials are more or less
direct in nature with little or no analysis like the names of Lord Krsna, saintly
qualities and analogies. Also, quick overviews of somé crucial characters that appear
About Bhágavata Subodhini
throughout the canto(s) are presented. This section aims at providing an interesting,
quick yet extensive reference guide.
NOMENCLATURE
To keep the reading of Bhágavata Subodhini relatively simpie, we've used the
referencing for verses in the standard "canto, chapter and verse number" formát. The
following short forms represent their equivalent expanded forms given against them.
•    SB - Srimad Bhágavatam
•    C C - Sri Caitanya Caritámrta
•    BRS - Bhakti Rasámrta Sindhu
•    CB - Caitanya Bhágavata
•    3.2.6 - SB Canto Three, Chapter Two, Verse Six
•    3.2.6 P - Srila Prabhupáda's purport on 3.2.6
•   3.2.6 V - Srila Visvanátha Cakravarti tháküra's commentary on 3.2.6
References to other books would be stated distinctively.
We humbly bow down to all the Vaisnavas and seek their blessings that this book -
with all its faults in presentation, language and grammar - is able to inspire one to
study Srimad-Bhágavatam. Srila Prabhupáda had quoted the following verse in his
introduction to the Bhágavatam; with faith that this book-form of Lord Krsna will
bring light into the darkness of Kali-yuga and dissipate the ignorance in our hearts.
tad-vág-visargo  janatdgha-viplavo
yasmin prati-slokam abaddhavaty api
ndmdny anantasya yaso 'nkitdni yac
chrnvanti gdyanti grnanti sddhavah
"On the other hand, that literature which is full of descriptions of the
transcendental glories of the name, fame, forms, pastimes, etc, of the unlimited
Supreme Lord is a different creation, full of transcendental words directed toward
bringing about a revolution in the impious lives of this world's misdirected
civilization. Such transcendental literatures, even though imperfectly composed,
are heard, sung and accepted by purified men who are thoroughly honest." [1.5.11)
We pray that our humble attempt in presenting this Bhágavata Subodhini
pleases Srila Prabhupáda, the ácáryas and the Vaisnava community.

A B O U T T H E THIRD C A N T O
Srimad-Bhágavatam, the ripened fruit of the tree of Vedic wisdom compiled by Srila
Vyásadeva at the maturity of his knowledge, is non-different from the Supreme Lord,
Sri Krsna. It is the granthávatara   of the Lord that narrates the pastimes of various
avatáras of the Lord. Also glorified as a mahá-purána, it is rich with the descriptions
of the ten subjects beginning with sárga.
atra sargo visargas ca sthánam posanam     ütayah
manvantaresánukathd     nirodho muktir dsrayah
In the Srimad-Bhágavatam there are ten divisions of statements regarding the
following: the creation of the universe, subcreation, planetary systems,
protection by the Lord, the creative impetus, the change of Manus, the science
of God, returning home, back to Godhead, liberation, and the summum
bonum. [SB 2.10.1]
Srila Jiva Gosvámi has explained that the ten principal topics of Srimad-Bhágavatam
are found within each of the twelve cantos. He also mentioned that one should not
assign each of the ten topics to a particular canto. Nor should the Srimad-
Bhágavatam be artificially interpreted to show that it deals with the topics
successively. The simple fact is that all the aspects of knowledge important to humán
beings, summarized in the ten categories mentioned above, are described with various
degrees of emphasis and analysis throughout the Srimad-Bhágavatam. (SB 12.7.9-10
Purport). Although a specific topic is emphasized prominently in a particular canto of
the Bhágavatam, the other topics are also discussed in those cantos, but with
relatively lesser emphasis. A careful study will reveal that sárga is prominently found
in the Third canto, visarga in the Fourth canto, sthdnam in the Fifth canto, posana in
the Sixth canto, üti in the Seventh canto, manvantara in the Fighth canto, isdnukathd
in the Ninth canto, nirodha in the Twelfth canto, mukti in the Fleventh canto and
asraya in the Tenth canto. The prominence of the ten topics in particular cantos is
confirmed by Srila Prabhupáda and Srila Visvanátha Cakravarti Thákura. And
according to Sríla Jiva Gosvámi, the first two cantos discuss at least sárga, visarga,
posana and certainly ásraya.
T h e First canto of the Bhágavatam introduces the key philosophical elements like
Bhakti,   Bhagaván,   Bhakta    and   Bhágavatam      that   forms   the   foundational
understanding of a devotee and also the prominent characters of Srimad-Bhágavatam,
namely Pariksit Mahárája, Sukadeva Gosvámi, Srila Vyásadeva, Nárada Muni,
Vidura, Lord Krsna etc, along with their backgrounds, thus setting the stage for
unfolding of this great literature. Right at the onset, there are descriptions of the
Absolute Truth and the highest (purpose of) religion. And there is glorification of the
Bhágavatam throughout this canto in various contexts. The six questions by the sages
and their answers by Srila Süta Gosvámi (1.1-3) establish the impottance of Bhakti,
Bhagavan and Bhagavatam - devotional service as the supreme occupation of a
humán being. Lord Krsna as the Supreme Personality of Godhead and the
fountainhead of innumerous incarnations, and Bhágavatam as the ultimate shelter of
religion after Krsna's disappearance. The rest of the canto majorly deals with
answering the key questions asked by the sages about the history of Bhágavatam, the
birth and activities of Pariksit Mahárája and his meeting with Sukadeva Gosvámi.
While presenting the history of the appearance of Bhágavatam (1.4-7), Süta Gosvámi
also gives the backstories of Srila Vyásadeva and Nárada Muni and the parampará of
the Bhágavatam. Along with the description of the events leading to the birth of
Pariksit (1.12), we find the prayers of queen Kunti (1.8) and the departure and prayers
of Bhisma (1.9) that announce the glory of a surrendered devotee. The chapters that
describe Lord Krsna's departure from Hastinápura (1.10) and entrance into Dváraká
(1.11) reveal the feelings of vipralambha   and sambhoga of the residents and even
introduces mádhurya    rasa of the queens of Dváraká. Another important character,
Vidura is introduced in 1.13 and the sages' inquiry about Maitreya-Vidura samváda
related to this chapter led to the Third and Fourth cantos. The canto progresses with
descriptions of the departure of the Supreme Lord Krsna (1.14), the retirement of the
virtuous Pándavas (1.15), the advent of evil Kali (1.16-17) and the renunciation of able
king Pariksit (1.18-19) showing the necessity of the Srimad-Bhágavatam and the
circumstances in which this granthávatara      made its appearance. At the end of
this canto, we get the most important questions about life asked by Pariksit. Thus
mm       Bhagavata Subodhini                                  ^/jj^^MM^^^^J^^Ű&J^^
the First Canto with all its wonderful aspects serves as an excellent introduction to
this great transcendental text Srimad-Bhágavatam.
T h e Second canto, beginning chapters (2.1-3) criticize the grhamedhl and present
Virát-rüpa and Paramátmá forms of the Lord as the objects of meditation for the
attached and detached yogis respectively and establish the supremacy of Bhakti yoga
over the other processes such as astdnga-yoga, karma-kdnda and demigod worship.
The questions by Pariksit mahárája (2.4) and Nárada Muni (2.5) about creation begin
the topic of sárga. The prayers of Sukadeva Gosvámi (2.4) and the talks of Lord
Brahmá (2.5-7) that include the descriptions of Kárana srsti (creation of 23 elements
- activities of Káranodakasáyi Visnu), Kárya srsti (assembly of elements - activities
of Garhhodakasáyi Visnu), Virát-rüpa (details of the universe and social orders) and
various avatáras (Lord's entrance into the created universe) reveal once again (both
directly and rhetorically) that Lord Krsna is the Supreme Lord and the originál source
of everything that exists. Here Brahmá gives special prominence to the position and
the activities of Lord Krsna and humbly declares his subordinate position. The
answers to the variety of questions asked by Pariksit, in 2.8, form the rest of
Bhágavatam. Sukadeva Gosvámi answers somé of them through the catuh-sloki
Bhágavatam (2.9) that contains the sambandha, abhideya and prayojana tattvas and
somé more through the description of the universal form. Thus the Second canto,
surveys several processes of the Vedic landscape and ties them together as ultimately
leading to Lord Krsna and Krsna Bhakti. And the ten topics of Bhágavatam
mentioned in 2.10 form another introduction like a map to the contents of the
Bhágavatam.
Thus the first two cantos together make an excellent prelude to the rest of the
Bhágavatam. According to Padma Purána, these two cantos correspond to the Lord's
lotus feet. And the third and the fourth cantos correspond to His thighs.
T h e T h i r d canto of Srimad Bhágavatam begins with Pariksit mahárája's inquiry
about the momeutous meeting of Vidura and Maitreya. I n the beginning, Pariksit
glorifies Vidura's inquiries as highly purposeful and approved by the learned; and so
are the answers of Maitreya, as acknowledged repeatedly by Vidura himself. Their
striking conversation spans up till the end of the fourth canto.
The beauty of the third canto lies in the variegated rangé of topics that it covers and
the awe-inspiring characters of great personalities that it describes. The topics include
About The Third Canto
creation, subcreation, time, embryology, family life, Sánkhya, bhakti yoga, astanga
yoga, jhána yoga, karma, culture, spiritual world, matériái world and hell. These
topics are interwoven in the stories, prayers and conversations of illustrious
personalities like Vidura, Uddhava, Maitreya, Brahmá, Kumáras, Jaya, Vijaya,
Sváyambhuva Manu, Devahüti and Kardama. While Lord Varáha's pastimes (3.13,
17-19) create special exhilaration, the teachings of Lord Kapila (3.25-33) will provide
one of the most deep philosophical experiences for the reader/ student of the
Bhágavatam.
The canto begins with the descriptions of Vidura's detachment and maturity (3.1); his
immense eagerness to hear Krsna-kathá is seen throughout the canto. Uddhava's deep
spiritual emotions for his beloved Lord Krsna and his amazing recoUection of Krsna's
pastimes and qualities (3.2-4) give us a glimpse not only into his devotion, but also
the sweetness of the tenth canto as a sample. Vidura's humility is evident in his
inclination to accept Uddhava, though younger, as his spiritual master. And
Uddhava's sensitivity and etiquette are laudable in not accepting the respectable elder
Vidura as a disciple, but directing him to Maitreya.
Humble Vidura's inquiries to Maitreya begin with the topics relevant for the
common man, rather than on an esoteric pláne (3.5). Maitreya descrihes the
magnificent creation of the universe originating from Mahá Visnu, His expansion
into it as Garhhodakasáyi Visnu and the manifestation of the Virát-rüpa, and at the
end, he humbly declares his inability to describe the Lord's unlimited glories (3.5-6).
Vidura's profound questions in 3.7 about various topics resemble the questions of
Pariksit in 2.8 and the answers to these questions are found in the entire Bhágavatam.
In 3.8, Maitraya describes the fascinating events of Brahmá's life - his birth, search
for Absolute truth, meditation and the beautiful darsana of Garhhodakasáyi Visnu.
Brahmá's prayers and the Lord's response (3.9) reveal the glory of the combination of
devotee's service attitűdé and the Lord's empowerment. Lurther, we see the richness
of the divisions of the matériái creation and the time factor (3.10-11) and the variety
in Brahmá's creation, visarga (3.12, 20). Along with the descriptions of the thrilling
pastimes   of   Lord   Varába   (3.13,17-19),   the   background of     Hiranyáksa   and
Hiranyakasipu as the doorkeepers of Vaikuntha, who entered the womb of Diti after
being cursed by Kumáras is given (3.14-16). These chapters describe the dangers of
lust and envy (3.14) by comparing them with the description of Vaikuntha (3.15)
where the residents are free from matériái lust and envy.
Bhagavata Subodhiní
In contrast to the household life of Kasyapa and Diti (3.14) that led to a demoniac
progeny, this canto also describes the ideál household life of Kardama and Devahüti
(3.21-24) that led to the appearance of Lord Kapiladeva. The ideál examples of
Sváyambhuva Manu as a father and ksatriya ruler, Kardama as a bráhmana sage and
Devahüti as a daughter, wife and mother are also described (3.21-24). Lord Kapila's
teachings (3.25-33) to His mother, Devahüti, open doors to a different rangé of
topics. His teachings, in essence repeatedly glorify the path of Bhakti yoga while
comparing and contrasting it to other processes like astánga yoga and jnána yoga.
Within these teachings are descriptions of - jiva (living entity), prakrti (matériái
nature) in which all the elements are described in terms of Sánkhya philosophy,
purusa (Suprme Lord), kála (time factor) and samsrti (continual birth and death of
living entity in 3.31-32), various classes of bhakti, limb-by-limb description of Lord's
form, good and had association and embryology. This section culminates in
Devahüti's prayers to Lord Kapila (3.33) that highlight the glory of the holy name,
and finally the perfection attained by Devahüti.
In this volume of Bhágavata Subodhini, the third canto is presented in a simple and
lucid manner to assist the reader in understanding it. Lspecially the technical aspects
of sárga, visarga and Lord's Kapila's teachings are presented with simplified tables
and flow charts in the Overview section; other vitai topics are presented in the
thematic compilations section. The special verses section and appendix section will
enhance the readers' appreciation for third canto.

3.4 Vidura Approaches Maitreya
1-3: DISAPPEARANCE OF YADUS
1. W i t h the Lord's permission (tad-anujnata), Yadus ate the remnants and also
drank liquor. They became delirious and used barsb words at eacb otber.
2. Due to intoxication, tbeir minds became unbalanced, and tbey destroyed eacb
otber, just as bamboos by mutual friction create fire and destroy tbe wbole forest.
L i n k : How could the eternal associates of the Lord be vanquished in His presence?
3. Tbis is an action of Lord's máyá (svátma-mdyáyd).    Witnessing tbis. Lord Krsna,
sipped tbe water of tbe River Sarasvati and sat down under a tree.
Note: The devatas who entered into various Yádavas, the Lord's eternal associates,
separated tbemselves from those Yádavas, and came to Prabhása-ksetra. Krsna
wanted them to be dispatched to their respective abodes as the mission of His descent
was accomplished. Further, it was a convincing display for the common people to
think that the Yádavas were destroyed by killing each other.
4. Lord Krsna is tbe vanquisber of distress of surrendered souls         (prapannárti-harena).
Previously (in Dváraká), He told me to go to Badarikásrama.
Note A: Krsna spoke to Uddhava for two reasons (both reasons reflect His quality of
being prapannárti-harena) - (i) He wanted to remove Uddhava's suffering of
separation after the disappearance of the Lord and His family, and (ii) He told
Uddhava to go Badarikásrama to relieve the pain of the sages there, by speaking
about bhakti, jnana, vairágya and the Lord's pastimes [Ref. 3.4.30])
Note B: When the purpose of Krsna's descent (to remove the burden of the earth)
had been fulfilled, the demigods headed by Brahmá came to Dváraká to inform Lord
Krsna that the time for His disappearance had arrived. Realizing the imminent
departure of His beloved Lord, Uddhava approached Krsna and requested that he
may also be allowed to go to spiritual world along with Krsna. Then Krsna spoke to
Uddhava the deepest philosophical section of the Bhágavatam, namely Uddhava-gttá
and finally instructed Uddhava to go to Badarikásrama. (Ref. 11.6 and 11.29).
5. Yet, in spite of knowing His desire, being unable to bear separation from His lotus
feet (páda-vislesandksamah),     I followed H i m (from Dváraká to Prabbása).
6. Searching for my Lord, I saw H i m deeply thinking alone, taking shelter on tbe
bank of tbe River Sarasvati altbougb He is tbe shelter of Laksmi        (sri-niketam).

7. I saw His beautiful form with a glowing dark complexion [syátndvadátath),               full
of bliss and knowledge (virajam),     peaceful reddisb eyes [prasdntdruna-locanam).            I
could recognize H i m as tbe Supreme Lord by His four bands [dorbhis caturbhir) and
yellow silk garments   [pita-kausdmbarena).
8. H i s sitting posture - resting against a young banyán tree (arbhaka asvattham              -
indicates tbis universe is tbe smallest of all; matériái energies are kept to His back)
witb His rigbt lotus foot on His left tbigb. Giving up His pastimes in tbis world
(tyakta-pippalam), He was blissful.
9. At tbat time Maitreya Rsi, a mahd-bhdgavata and tbe friend of Vyása, reacbed
tbere out of bis own perfect accord (Tbe meeting of Maitreya, wbo is not a pure
devotee, witb tbe Lord, is due to bis accidental devotional service or       ajnata-sukrti).
10. Maitreya was attached to Lord Krsna and listening to H i m submissively. Witb a
smile and a particular glancé upon me, having allowed me to rest, tbe Lord spoke as
follows.
Note: Although both Uddhava and Maitreya were great souls, Krsna was more
intimate and attentive towards Uddhava as he was a spotlessly pure devotee while
Maitreya at that time was a jndni-bhakta - one whose devotion is mixed with
monistic viewpoint.
Lord Krsna spoke to Uddhava:
11. O Vasú, I know your beart's desire (manasipsitarh)       for My association           (mat-
siddhi-kdtnena) witb wbicb you worsbiped Me in a sacrifice. I will award you tbat
rare benediction wbicb is difficult for others.
Note: Uddhava is an eternal associate (nitya-siddha) oí the Lord and a plenary
portion of Uddhava was one of the eight Vasus (sddhana-siddha) who entered the
nitya-siddha Uddhava. Here Uddhava is revealed as sddhana-siddha to protect the
confidentiality of his pastimes. Also the Lord is indicating to Maitreya, the
importance of association of the Lord.
12. Your present life is tbe last; now you can go to Vaikuntha. Your visit to Me in
tbis lonely place, wben I am about to disappear from tbe vision of tbis world, is
because of your pure devotional service and is a great boon for you.
13. At tbe beginning of tbe creation, I spoke unto Brabmá about M y transcendental
glories, wbicb tbe sages describe as Srimad-Bhágavatam      [catuh-sloki).

3.4 Vidura Approaches Maitreya
29. Sukadeva Gosvami: Lord Krsna desired to disappear from the earth after
dispatching His excessively numerous family members in tbe plea of tbe sages' curse.
Note: Krsna does not give up His body like the ordinary conditioned souls. His hody
and His self are identical. He appears and disappears out of His own will and potency
as repeatedly confirmed hy the scriptures and acaryas.

situated.

3.6 Creation Of The Universal Form
L i n k : At this stage of the universal   creation (at the end of 3.5), the 23 elements
namely, mahat-tattva, ahankdra, five tan-mdtras, five gross elements, ten senses and
the mind are existing       in an unmixed state. Therefore     the creative activities   are
suspended. The predominant deities of all these elements offered prayers to the Lord
(3.5.39-51). In 3.6 Maitreya    describes how Lord Kdranodakasdyi       Visnu responds to
those prayers by entering into the universe as Garbhodakasdyi        Visnu, and manifests
the Virdt-rüpa (universal     form).
1. Lord Karanodakasayi Visnu saw the creative functions suspended due to the non-
combination of His potencies (twenty-tbree elements).
2-3. Tbereafter, tbe Lord sent His Káli energy, tbe time potency or tbe bibhrat-sakti,
or tbe cohesive energy, wbicb amalgamates all tbe different elements. Tben, all tbe
living entities including tbe demigods are enlivened as if awakening from sleep.
4-5. Tbus set into action by tbe will of tbe Lord, tbe twenty-tbree elements produced
tbe gigantic universal form (Adhipürusa or Visva-rüpa or Virdt-purusa) wbicb is tbe
resting place for all tbe planetary systems and all movable and immovable creations.
6. Tbe Virdt-purusa or Hiranmaya (like foetus) witb all tbe living entities tesided for
tbousand celestial years on tbe water within tbe universe.
7. Tbe Virdt-purusa, or tbe universal foetus consists of tbe totál energy of tbe mahat-
tattva, divided Himself by Himself into (i) jiva-sakti - consciousness of living
entities, (ii) prdna-sakti - life airs and (iii) dtmd-sakti - self-identification wbicb are
subdivided into one, ten and tbree respectively.
8. Tbis Virdt-purusa is tbe first avatdra of Garbbodakasáyi Visnu and is tbe Self of
unlimited living entities and tbey rest on H i m .
L i n k 7 -> 9: The one, ten and three are described.
9. Tbe gigantic universal form is represented by tbree (adhydtma - body, mind and
senses; adhidaiva - controlling demigods of tbe senses; adhibhüta - gross organs
along witb sense objects), ten (ten kinds of life energy) and one (tbe beart wbere life
energy is generated).
10.  Tbe Lord as tbe Supersoul, remembering tbe prayers of tbe demigods,
manifested tbe virdt-rüpa by His own energy.

1-25: BRAHMÁ'S PRAYERS FOR CREATIVE IMPETUS
1. Supreme knowable object
•   After years of penance, I am now able to know Y o u by Your mercy.
•   People who do not know You are unfortunate.
•   Nothing is comparable with Y o u . Impure objects appear superior to You because
of transformation of matériái modes of máyá.
2. T h e originál form - Your rüpam      ...
•   Manifested by Your internál potency {avabodha-rasodayena),
•   Always freed from matériái contamination {sasvat nivrtta tamasah),
•   For the sake of devotees   (sat-anugraháya),
•   Origin (ádau) I Root of hundreds of incarnations {avatára sata-eka-bijam),
•   From whose navel the lotus is sprouted on which I am born {yat        nábhi-padma
bhavanát aham ávirásam)
3. Supreme personal form - Your impersonal Brahman effulgence       (ánanda-mátram)
is without changes (avikalpam) or deterioration of potency (aviddha-varcah). But I
(proud of my body and senses) surrender unto Your transcendental personal form
{bhavatah svarüpam), which includes Brahman.
L i n k : Why surrender to a personal form only?
4. Most auspicious form - Sri Krsna's forms create auspiciousness for all the
universes (bhuvana-mangala mangaláya) and are manifested for the devotees. Persons
destined for hell (naraka-bhágbhih) neglect these personal forms and speculate on
matériái topics (asat-prasatigaih).
5. Dweller of devotees' hearts (who smell through ears) - Y o u are never separated
from the devotees' hearts who smell through their earholes, the aroma of Your lotus
feet, carried by the air of Vedic sound and accept those feet as the greatest treasure
through bhakti.
ye tu       tvadiya-caranámbuja-kosa-gandham
jighranti karna-vivaraih     sruti-váta-nitam

6. Misidentification causes anxiety - As long as they do not take shelter of Your
safe lotus feet [anghrim abhayam), people imbued with the aham mameti conception,
are full of anxieties (árti-mülam)   for protecting their wealth, body, friends, etc.
L i n k : Then why doesn't everyone take to devotion?

•   Turnéd against the auspicious topics about You {bhavatah prasangdt)
•   Lngage in inauspicious activities {akusaláni) of temporary sense gratification
8. Miserable jivas are pitiable - M y mind is aggrieved for the poor creatures
constantly perplexed by hunger, thirst, kapha-pitta-vata, blasting beat, severe cold,
wind and rain, strong sex desire, indefatigable anger and other disturbing elements.

9. Bodily Identification binds - As long as the jiva, being influenced by mdyd, sees
the hody as a means for sense enjoyment, he cannot get out of matériái miseries,
which otberwise have no factual existence.
10. Lrustration of Nondevotees
•    Lngage senses in distressing work
•    Suffer insomnia due to various mentái speculations      (ndnd-manoratha-dhiyá)
•    Lrustrated in their plans {dhata-artha racanáh)
•    Lven jnanis continue in samsdra, if they are averse to Your topics.
Note: 3.9.7 and 10 describe the fallen position of the nondevotees who do not take
shelter of the process of hearing about the Lord - distress, frustration and matériái
miseries.
11. Dweller of devotees' hearts (who see through ears) - Y o u mercifully reside in
the lotus-like hearts of devotees, which are infused with bhakti-yoga. They see You
through the ears (by hearing), in the form they always particularly think of.

c. Nondevotees - unavailable
13. Actions offered to the Lord benefit - Activities like Vedic rituals, charity, severe

satisfy Y o u never go in vain.
14. Brahman (worsbiped by jnánis) - realized by intelligence for self-realization.

15. M e r c i f u l holy names - One who chants the holy names of the Lord, whose
incarnations, qualities and activities are mysterious, even unconsciously at the time of
death, is freed of all sins and attains H i m .
16. T h e cosmic tree - Y o u are the prime root of the tree of the cosmic manifestation
{bhuvana-drumáya),     which has three trunks - as me, Siva and Y o u , and expands
with multiple hranches.
17-18. T h e time factor - As long as the tendency for foolish work remains powerful
in people, all their plans will be cut to pieces, by powerful time. Although I am the
leader of all planets, performed years of austerities and stay for two parárdhas, still I
fear Your time factor.
Note: parárdha = 4,300,000,000 x 30 x 12 x 100 solar years.
19. Various transcendental forms - Y o u appear in various species, among animals,
humans and demigods to perform transcendental pastimes and also to fuifill the
obligations of Your own principles {átma-krta-setu).
20-21. Garbbodakasáyi V i s n u - You are happily sleeping {yoganidrá) on the snake
bed, in the violent waters of devastation with all the universes stationed within Your
abdomen. I am born from the lotus sprouted from Your navel. Now at the end of the
night of devastation, after finishing Your sleep, Y o u open Your eyes, which hloom
like lotuses. [Please wake up! I, Your servant, should perform mangala-árati for you.]
22-25: Requests to the Lord
22. Empower me - Y o u , endowed with six opulences, are the friend and maintainer
of all [samasta-jagatám  suhrd). Please empower me, with the introspection to create,
for I am also one of the surrendered souls (pranata).
23. Protect me from falsé ego - You are the benefactor of surrendered souls. When I
create this universe endowed with Your power, I wish to not be materially affected
by my works and ahle to give up the falsé prestige of being the creator.

Vedic hymns [ma ririsista nigamasya girám visargah).
25. Please wake up - May the Lord who is the Supreme, the oldest and unlimitedly
merciful, smilingly hestow His benediction by opening His lotus eyes. He can uplift

devastation, the Lord spoke in deep, thoughtful words, removing the illusion.

1-3: VIDURA'S INQUIRIES ABOUT BRAHMÁ'S CREATION
1-2. V i d u r a inquired: H o w did Brahmá, the grandfather of all planetary inhabitants
(loka-pitámahah)      create the bodies of living entities from his own body and mind?
Kindly eradicate all my doubts, and answer all my inquiries.
3. Being enlivened, Maitreya began to reply the questions, which he had kept in his
heart (did not forget).
Canto 3 Overview    ^MMMM^MMMMMMMMMMM.
4-9: LORD BRAHMA CREATES THE FOURTEEN WORLDS
4. Brahmá meditated on the Lord for hundred celestial years as advised by the Lord.
5. He saw both the lotus and the water trembling due to a strong, violent wind.
6. Due to long penance (tapa) and transcendental knowledge (vidyá) [as told by the
Lord in 3.9.30], lord Brahmá acquired practical knowledge (vivrddha-vijnána)  and
drank the wind and the water completely.
7. Brahmá then saw that the lotus spread throughout the universe, and contemplated,
"Lrom this lotus, I will create all the planets as they were before the destruction."
8. Thus engaged in the Lord's service, Brahmá entered into the whorl of the lotus and
first divided it into three divisions and later into fourteen divisions.
9. Brahmá could create all the fourteen worlds suitable for different types of jlvas
because of his causeless devotional service in mature transcendental knowledge.
L i n k : Maitreya    had spoken about 'time' several times previously    in 3.8.12, 3.8.13,
3.8.22 and 3.10.5. Therefore, Vidura now asks about time.
10. Vidura asked, "Describe the symptoms of eternal time, which is another form of
the L o r d . "
11-13:   CHARACTERISTICS OF TIME
(i)   Source of the interactions of modes     (guna-vyatikarákáro)
(ii)   Lack of attributes and hence imperceptible (nirviseso)
(iii)   Limitless - without beginning or end (apratisthitah)
(iv)    (As an instrumental cause,) Separates the matériái energy from the Lord
(visvam isvarena paricchinnath)
(v)   Divides the universe into various planetary systems (visvath paricchinnath)
(vi)   Unmanifested, impersonal feature of Lord       (avyakta-mürtiná)
(vii)   Objective manifestation or representative of Lord       (brahma-tan-mátrath)
(viii)   Causes the universe to appear alike in the present, as it was in the past and shall
be in the future (tdrsam).
L i n k : Having described time in generál, Maitreya    will describe time in detail in the
next chapter (3.11). Before that he discusses the process of creation in summary.

27. Creation of sense devatas (from ahankdra in sattva-guna) comes under prakrta.
Creation of other devatás by Brahmá comes under vaikrta.
Creation of the four Kumáras is mixed creation (both prákrta and vaikrta), as they
were generated by Brahmá's meditation on the Lord (thus both Brahmá and the Lord
are the cause).
30. I shall describe the manvantaras and the descendants of the Manus. The creator,
Brahmá, as the incarnation of the passión mode of the Lord, creates the universal
affairs with unfailing desires (amogha-sankalpa) by the force of the Lord's energy.

-15: CALCULATION OF TIME FROM DIVISIONS OF DISTANCE
Link: Finding the nature of subtle time difficult        to describe, Maitreya first describes
the divisions of matter (3.11.1-4).
Note: Time and space are two correlative terms. Time is measured in terms of its
covering a certain space of atoms by the movement of the sun (3.11.4 P).
1. Paramanu (atom) —> matériái manifestation's ultimate smallest partiele, which is
indivisible and invisible. They are innumerable such particles.
2. Parama-mahán —> totality of all the paramdnus (greatest partiele of matter).
3. Just as matter has small and big divisions, time also has similar divisions. Time is
the potency of the all-pervading Lord.

15. One should worship the sun once every five years because he (i) enlivens with
his beat and light, (ii) diminishes life duration and thus releases from matériái
illusion and (iii) enlarges the path of elevation to heaven.
THE KALPA OF BRAHMA
16. 1 understand the lifespans of beings in Svarga, Pitá and earthly planets. Please
describe the life span of others (like Bhrgu) who are beyond the kalpa of Brahmá.
17. Because you are a self-realized person, you can understand the movements of
eternal time and can see everything by the power of mystic vision.
L i n k : The life spans of those beyond a kalpa are equal to that of Brahmá.   In order to
explain the lifespan of Brahmd, Maitreya first explains the calculation ofthe yugas.
18-22:   DURATION OF YÜI
18.1 Catur-yuga (Satya, Tretá, Dvápara and Kali) = 12,000 years of the demigods.
19. Duration of the four yugas
Kali                    1,200                 432,000
Dvápara                 2,400                 864,000
Tretá                   3,600                 1,296,000
Satya                  4,800                 1,728,000
1 catur-yuga (totál)    12,000               4,320,000

Canto 3 Overvie
20. A yuga during which particular yuga-dharmas are practiced, is the time duration
between two yuga-sandhyds (transitional periods).
21. I n Satya-yuga, dharma was completely maintained, but it gradually decreased by
VZ part in every next yuga while adharma is proportionately admitted.
22. Above Svargaloka (Maharloka to Brahmaloka), the duration of one day = 1000
catur-yugas; the night during which Brahmá sleeps is also of the same duration.
23-27:   DURATION OF MANUS        ^/jjjj^/Z/Z
23. At the end of Brahmá's night, the creation of the planets (from Svargaloka to
Pátálaloka) takes place and stays through the lifespans of fourteen Manus.
24. Ruling time of 1 Manu = little more than 71 catur-yugas. (852,000 demigod years
or 306,720,000 humán years)
25. In a manvantara, (i) seven Sages, (ii) demigods, (iii) Indra, and (iv) Manu appear
simultaneously, but (v) Manu's descendants appear gradually.
Note: There are six elements of a manvantara namely manu, manu-putras, devatas,
sapta-rsis, suresvara, and amsávatdra of the Lord (Ref. 12.7.15). Canto 8 describes
the six elements of all the fourteen manvantaras of the current kalpa of Brahmá.
26. I n the daily creation of Brahmá, the three planetary systems revolve, and their
residents appear and disappear according to their karmas.
27. During the manvantaras, the Lord appears in different incarnations [manvantara
avatdras), as Manu and others and maintains the universe.
28-32:   DESTRUCTION AT THE END OF KALPA
28. The Lord, in the form Kalagni-rudra, destroys the three worlds at the end of
Brahmá's day. The jivas remain mérged in that dissolution, and everything is silent.
29. At that time, the three worlds disappear and the sun and moon are without glare.
30. During this time, the residents of Maharloka, distressed by the fire emanating
from the mouth of Sankarsana, move to Janaloka.
31. At the beginning of devastation all the oceans swell and cover the three worlds.
32. Garhhodakasáyi Visnu remains in that water, lying on Ananta, while being
glorified by the residents of Janaloka.

33-37:   LIFESPAN OF BRAHMA
33. Thus, days and nights pass even for Brahma and his life also comes to an end.
One's life endures for only 100 years, in terms of the times in different planets.
34. Parárdha - Half of Brahmá's life (50 years). The first parárdha                        is already over
and the second has begun currently.
35. Bráhma-kalpa - The first day in the first parárdha oí Brahmá's life (also called
Sveta-varáha-kalpa), during which Brahmá is born along with the four Vedas.
36. Pádma-kalpa - The next kalpa which followed the first kalpa (also called Pitr-
kalpa), in which the universal lotus comes from the navel of Garhhodakasáyi Visnu.
37. Varáha-kalpa - The first day of the second parárdha                       of Brahmá's life, during
which Lord H a r i appeared as Boar.
IN FRONT OF THE LORD                                                 \
38. This dvi-parardha (entire life of Brahmá) is only a nimesa (less than a second) for
the Lord, who is unchanging (avyákrtasya),        unlimited (anantasya), beginningless
(anádeh) and the soul of the universe    (jagad-átmanah).
39. Time is the controller of anyone who is bodily conscious, with life spans between
paramanu and dvi-parardha. But, time cannot control the Supreme Lord.
40-41. The universes with 500 millión yojanas (4 biliion miles; 1 yojana = 8 miles)
diameter and the layers of elements covering the universes each ten times thicker than
the one before, looks as small as a paramanu when they come out of the bodily pores
of Mahá-visnu.
42. Sri Krsna is therefore the originál cause of all causes. Thus the spiritual abode of
Mahá-visnu is eternal and supreme.

,     r'^Vfj V . •           1-3:   BRAHMA FIRST CREATES IGNORANCE
1. Maitreya - I described to you about time. Now hear about Brahmá's creation.
L i n k : Unless a living entity forgets his real identity as the eternal servant of            Lord
Krsna, it is impossible for him to live in matériái          conditions of life. So, Brahmd first
created ignorance as a matter of necessity.
2 . Lord Brahmá created the 5 features of ignorance               (ajhdna-vrttis)
sasarjdgre 'ndha-támisram       atha támisram ádi-krt
mahdmoham ca mohám ca           tamás cdjhdna-vrttayah
(i)   tamah     Darkness in self-knowledge or self deception (ignorance of svarüpa)
(ii)   mohám —>• Illusory bodily conception (forgetfulness of one's real identity)
(iii)   mahd-moham      Falsé proprietorship of enjoyable objects (possessiveness)
(iv)    támisram —>• Anger upon frustration (when one's enjoyment is obstructed)
(v)    andha-támisram  -+ Unconscious state brought about by anger (sense of death)
3. Seeing the sinful nature of his creation (pápiyasím srstirii), Brahmá was displeased.
He then purified his mind by medirating on the Lord and again began the creation.
4-6: CREATION OF THE FOUR KUMÁRAS
4. Brahma created the four Kumaras who were free of fruitive actions (niskriyan) as
their semen flowed upwards   (ürdhva-retasah).
Note: The four aspects of vidyá (knowledge) namely sánkhya, yoga, vairágya and
tapas appeared in the form of Sanaka, Sananda, Sanátana and Sanat-kumára
respectively. Since vidyá is useless without bhakti, bhakti also is present in them, but
as a secondary item.
5. Brahmá asked the Kumáras to create progeny, but they refused as they were
devoted to Lord Vásudeva (vásudeva-paráyanáh) and aimed only at liberation.
6. T h u s disrespected by his sons, Brahmá was angered, but he tried to control it.
7 - 2 0 : CREATION OF RÚDRA
7. Despite Brahmá's efforts to control his anger by intelligence, it came out from
between his eyebrows as a child with blue (ignorance) and red (passión) complexion
(nila-lohitah).
8. The child cried, " O destiny maker (dháta) and teacher of the universe (jagad-guru),
please designate my name and place."
^                     -            _.       __.                                          J-A^
9-10. Brahmá pacified the child hy accepting his request and said, " O best of the
demigods (sura-srestha), because you cried anxiously, your name will be Rúdra."
11. " I selected eleven places for y o u . " (Eleven residences of Rúdra principle or anger)
(i) Heart - falsé egocentric attitűdé of thinking oneself to be all in all.
(ii) Senses - the anger in the heart is manifested through senses
(iii) A i r of life - angry man breathes rapidly
(iv) Sky - dense clouds roar in anger
(v) A i r - wind hlows very fiercely
(vi) Water - flood; sea water infuriated by wind —>• appears gloomy
(vii) Fire - ablaze
(viii) E a r t h - transforms into weapons; inundation; earthly creatures like snake and
tiger
(ix) Sun - extrémé beat leads to heatstroke or even death
(x) Moon - extrémé cold leads to collapse or even death
(xi) Austerity (without bhakti) - somé yogis, philosophers and renunciants
12. " Y o u have eleven names - Manyu, Manu, Mahinasa, Mahán, Siva, Rtadhvaja,
Ugraretá, Bhava, Kála, Vámadeva and Dhrtavrata."
13. " Y o u have eleven wives called Rudránis - Dhi, Dhrti, Rasalá, Umá, Niyut, Sarpi,
Ilá, Ambiká, Irávati, Svadhá and Díksá."
14. "Accept these places and wives and create progeny on a large scale."
15-16. Rúdra created progeny similar to himself in strength (sattva), form (ákrti) and
furious nature (svabháva).    The unlimited progeny created by Rúdra attempted to
devour the entire universe and Brahmá became afraid.
17-19. Brahmá told Rúdra, "There is no need to create such living entities who
are devastating everything and even attacking me. Please perform austerity so that
you can (i) achieve auspiciousness, (ii) hestow happiness to all jivas, (iii) re-create
the universe as before and (iv) quickly know the Lord.
20. Rúdra agreed and went to the forest to perform austerity.

29-31. Seeing Brahmá so deluded, his sons, the sages headed by Marici spoke to him

(iii) Your character is followed by people in generál for spiritual improvement
32. As their efforts failed to dissuade Brahmá, they all prayed to the Supreme Lord
who created the cosmos, to protect dharma.
33. Hearing his Prajápati sons, Brahmá was ashamed of his act, and gave up his body
(mentality), which appeared in all directions as fog in darkness (nihdram).
Link: The status of exalted personalities is not contaminated by apparently audacious
transgression of morality. Brahmá's          power of creation did not diminish due to his
distraction because of Vák. Brahmá continued his creation         service.

48. Brahmá, whose body is composed of the Vedas, is invested with multifarious
energies and is the personal representation of the Supreme Lord.
49. Giving up his previous body, Brahmá accepted a new body in which sex life is not
forhidden and concentrated his mind on creation.
50-57:     CREATION OF SVÁYAMBHUVA MANU AND SATARÜPÁ
50-51. Seeing that the creation by the powerful sages did not sufficiently increase the
population of the universe, Brahmá reflected, " H o w astonishing! This misfortune is
because of destiny."
52. While Brahmá was thus deliberating, two forms (one male and one female) were
generated from his body. They were still celebrated as Brahmá's body (káya).
53-55. They were Sváyambhuva Manu and his queen Satarüpá who united and
gradually increased the population of the universe.
56. In due course of time, they begot five children - two sons, Priyavrata and
Uttánapáda, and three daughters, Áküti, Devahüti and Prasüti.
57. Áküti was married to the sage Ruci, Devahüti to Kardama, and Prasüti to Daksa.
From them, the world was fiiled with population.

equating him with the Supreme Lord, as he is the Lord's representative.                      In this way,
they enumerated the qualifications of Brahmá to solve the current                      problem.
3. Y o u are beyond tbe influence of time; therefore nothing is unknown to you.

wbo bave accepted a body by tbe Lord's máyá, composed of rajoguna.
6-7. A l l tbe planets exist within you, and all tbe jivas are generated from you. Persons

tbe jivas, just as a rope controls tamed bulls.

26-32:   THE LORD RECONCILES THE SITUATIONÍ
26. The Lord replied: The punishment on them, as becoming demons, was ordained
by Me. But tbey will sbortly return to Me as tbey will be absorbed in meditation on
Me in anger             {samrambha-sambhrta-samádhy-anubaddha-yogau).
Note: Nothing can happen without the Lord's sanction. The Lord's plan was behind
this curse. Because the Lord is the source of everything, anger and fighting spirit also
exist in Him. But in Vaikuntha, the Lord has no enemies to fight with, because
everyone is engaged in His service. So the Lord comes to the matéria) world along
with His devotees (in this case Jaya and Vijaya) who temporarily act as His enemies
to fuifill their beloved Lord's fighting desires.
Furthermore, while fulfilling His desires, the Lord instructed the sádhakas through
this episode - to be careful not to commit offenses.

1. After hearing from Brahmá, all the demigods gave up fear and returned to their
respective places.
2. Diti had been apprehensive of the trouble to the demigods from her children, so
she gave birth to the twins only after a hundred years.
3. After their birth, many fearful natural disturbances started to occur on the earth,
the heavens and the outer space.

15. Seeing these evil omens, everyone except the four Kumaras, are afraid and

15-19: THE     BROTHERS GREW UP
16. The two brothers began to grow like mountains with bodies as strong as steel.
17. With their gigantic forms they seemed to kiss the sky with their crowns and each
of their steps shook the earth, surpassed the sun with their belted waists.
18. Kasyapa gave them names - the first-born son was named Hiranyáksa, and the
first-conceived (born later) son was named Hiranyakasipu.
19. Fearless Hiranyakasipu having received a benediction from Brahmá started to
control all the three worlds.
J0-26iHiRANY^                          ALL DIRECTIONS
20. Hiranyáksa traveled all over the universe with a fighting spirit just to satisfy his
elder brother Hiranyakasipu.
21-22. Seeing his uncontrollable temper, gigantic form with golden anklets, garland
and huge mace, his mentái and bodily strength and the boon he received, all the
demigods got frightened and fled.
23. Seeing that the devatás had disappeared because of his strength, he roared loudly.
24. After returning from heaven, with a desire to sport, he dove into the ocean.
25. The aquatics that förmed the host of Varuna ran away with fear.
26. Moving about in the ocean for many years striking the waves with his mace, he
reacbed Vibhávari, the capital of Varuna.

27-31:   CONVERSATION BETWEEN VARUNA AND HIRANYÁKSA
27-28. There Hiranyáksa feli at Varuna's feet like a lowborn man, and to make fun
of him, he said, "Give me battle, O Supreme Lord! You are the guardian of an entire
sphere, a great ruler, a great warrior, and having conquered all the daityas and
dánavas, you once performed a Rájasüya sacrifice."
29. Thus provoked, Varuna controlled his anger using his intelligence and replied,
"We have now desisted from fighting, having grown too old for combat."
30-31. "Only Lord Visnu can satisfy your desire to fight. Lven heroes like you praise
His prowess. On reaching H i m you will be rid of your pride and will lie down on the
field of battle, surrounded by dogs, for eternal sleep. He assumes incarnations like
Varáha to exterminate wicked fellows like you and to show grace to the virtuous."

would be very much satisfied.

8. The Lord angrily replied Hiranyáksa with sharp words and laughed.

10. "Indeed, We are jungle creatures, and searching for dogs like you. One who is

promise to give Me the happiness of fighting. He who does not keep promise is unfit to

45-46. Creation of Kinnaras and Kimpurusas
45. Brahmá, beheld his o w n reflection, and a d m i r i n g himself, he created the K i n n a r a s
and K i m p u r u s a s out of that reflection.
46. T h e y accepted the body of reflection cast off by Brahmá. T h a t is w h y they sing
his praises i n the d a w n .
47-48: Creation of Snakes
47. N o t satisfied w i t h the pace of progress of creation, Brahmá l a y d o w n i n a sulién
mood and gave up that body.
48. F r o m that body and its hairs arose the snakes, serpents and m a n y hooded Nágas.
49-51: Creation of Manu and others
49. Brahmá, t h i n k i n g himself successful, created, f r o m his m i n d the M a n u s .
50. Brahmá gave to M a n u his o w n humán f o r m . O n seeing the M a n u s , those w h o
had been created e a r l i e r — t h e demigods, the G a n d h a r v a s etc.—applauded Brahmá.
51. T h e y p r a y e d : W e are glad. Since ritualistic acts have n o w been established
soundly i n this humán f o r m , w e shall a l l share the s a c r i f i c i a l oblations.
52-53: Creation of the Sages
52. Brahmá evolved great sages as his beloved sons b y equipping himself w i t h                  tapa,
vidyd, yoga, samddhi,       etc.
53. T o each one of these sons, Brahmá gave a part of his o w n body, w h i c h w a s
characterized      by deep         meditation,    mentái      concentration,       supernatural power,
austerity, adoration and renunciation.
Note: I n the 3.10, 12 and 20, sometimes the events of the secondary creation are in
sequence and sometimes somé elements are omitted or put in a different order. The
actual sequence of creation hy Brahmá as given hy Srila Visvanátha Cakravarti
Thákura is - (i) Animals with five joints (ii) Trees and Plants (iii) Snakes (iv) Cows
and Buffaloes (v) Yaksas, Ráksasas, Kinnaras and Kimpurusas (vi) Kumáras (vii)
Manus, humans.

35-37: Reason for My appearance

perfection and will he freed from fear.

1-3. Saunaka R s i : Please descrihe to us the activities of Lord Kapila as
•   He is unhorn (aja), yet took hirth as Lord Kapila hy His yogamáyá, to

6-11: DEVAHÜTI'S QUESTIONS To LORD KAPILA
6. Rememhering the words of Brahma, Devahüti hegan to question Lord Kapila who
could show her the ultimate goal of the Ahsolute Truth [tattva-márgágra-darsanam).
7. I am very sick of the disturhance caused due to material senses hy which I have
fallen into ignorance.
8. You are my transcendental eye {sat-caksur) and the one who makes me cross over
(páragam) this ignorance. After many hirths today I attained Y o u , hy Your mercy.
9. You have arisen like the sun to dissipate the darkness of ignorance of the
universe.
10. [ Q l ] Please dispel my illusion of 'aham mameti' due to my falsé ego and created
by Your maya.
Note: It is Your maya alone that has bewildered me and I should ask You only.
11.1 take Your shelter (saranam), hecause Y o u are the
(i) only person of whom to take shelter (saranyam).
(ii) axe, which can cut the tree of material existence of Your devotees (sva-bhrtya
samsdra-taroh kuthdram).
(iii) hest of the knowers of hhakti (sad-dharma-vidárh varistham)
So, please explain ahout the jiva in material world (purusa) and prakrti.^
Note: Purusa is the Supreme Lord or anyone who imitates Him as an enjoyer (jiva)
and tries to exploit prakrti. The intricacies in the material world of the relationship of
prakrti (enjoyed) and purusa (enjoyer), is called sarhsdra, or material entanglement.
' Lord Kapila describes about purusa and prakrti in 3.26.3-8. Devahüti asks Him to elaborate
on this topic in 3.26.9 [Q3a]. Lord Kapila then elaborates further in 3.26 and 27.
12-27:^^^WER T o Q l -          ^pj^^^JJ^J^^N^f^SSSSf
12. Hearing His mother's uncontaminated desire (niravadyam ipsitam) for increasing
the liheration of the humanity (puthsdm apavarga-vardhanam). Lord Kapila thanked
her mentally and explained the path of devotees (satám gatir).
13-15: Ádhyátmika-Yoga
13. I consider ádhyátmika-yoga (the yoga system concerning the soul), as the most
heneficial (nihsreyasdya) as it causes detachment (uparatih) from material happiness
and distress.
yoga ádhyátmikah pumsám mato nihsreyasdya me
atyantoparatir yatra duhkhasya ca sukhasya ca
Note: Ádhydtmika-yoga consists of three types - (i) Bhakti, (ii) Jnana (or Sahkhya)
and (iii) Astáhga-yoga (or yoga). This is the seed verse expanding these three topics
into eight chapters of sánkhya-yoga (3.25-32).
14. A l l the limbs of this ancient yoga system are easy to perform (yogam sarvdnga-
naipunam). I explained this to the great sages eariier, and now I will explain to you.
15. The mind is the pivot. If the mind is attached to material modes, it causes
hondage and if it is attached to the Supreme Lord it causes liheration.
16-18: Symptoms of liberated mind
16. When one's mind is cleansed of the impurities like lust and greed produced from
falsé ego, one transcends material happiness and distress.
17. At that time the soul can see himself to he transcendental, self-effulgent, nevet
fragmented, although very minute in size.
18. H o w does the mind reach this stage? - By bhakti endowed with jnána and
vairdgya, one sees everything in the right perspective (paripasyati). He hecomes

50. When all the elements were unmixed, the Supreme Lord entered the universe
along with kála, karma and gunas.

an unintelligent egg arose, from which appeared the virát-rüpa.
52. Within this universal egg is the universal form (14 planetary systems), which is
covered outside by seven layers. Fach outside layer is ten times thicker than the
previous layer. Final outside layer is covered by pradhána.
53. The virát-purusa situated Himself in that golden egg, which was lying on the
water, and He divided it into many departments.

62-69. The devatás then wanted to wake up the virát-purusa. But upon failing to do

L i n k : In 3.27, Kapila discusses the method of Bhakti-misra-jndna and the method for
attaining liheration [Answer to Q3].
1-5: JívA IN CONDITIONED STATE
1. The jiva, although situated in prakrti, is unaffected by its gunas, because he is
unchanging (avikárdd) and not the proprietor (akrtavdn), just as the sun remains
aloof from its reflection on the water.
2. When the same jiva becomes absorbed in the gunas oí prakrti and bewildered by
the falsé ego, he falsely thinks that he is the proprietor of everything.
3. Thus he performs faulty action and moves in saihsára (repeated hirth and death) in
good, had and mixed species of life.
4. Although transcendental, his troublesome material condition does not cease
hecause of his constant meditation on sense objects (hy mind), thinking himself to he
the doer.
5. Solution - to gradually control the materially attached mind through bhakti, yoga
with detachment (virakti).
6 - 1 1 : ATTAINING LIBERATION BY JNÁNA
6. Process - (i) Faithfully practice astanga-yoga [yama, niyama, etc), (ii) with
devotion and faith that I am real and (ii) hearing M y topics.
Note: In bhakti-misra jnána, which aims at impersonal liheration, the primary
element is jhána, and the secondary elements are bhakti and astanga-yoga.

15. Although he can feel his existence vividly, he thinks that he is lost and feels

17-19. [Q4a] Since prakrti and jiva are attracted to each other eternally, how is their
separation possihle? Although not the doer, he is attached to the modes and prakrti
acts on him and hinds him. Then, how is liheration possible?
20. Even if the great fear of hondage is sometimes avoided hy sánkhya (inquiry ahout
the fundamental principles), it may still reappear since its cause has not ceased.
21-23: KAPILA RECOMMENDS INTENSE PRACTICE
21-22. One can get liheration hy intense practice that includes -
•    intense bhakti for a long time (tivrayá bhaktya ciram)
•    without desire for fruitive results [animitta-nimittena)
•    hy executing prescribed duties (sva-dharmena)
•    nourishment hy hearing ahout Me (sruta-sambhrtayá)
•    perfect knowledge and detachment (jhánena,     vairágyena)

23. By thus practicing seriously, the influence of prakrti over jiva can he removed,

C l 1-12:   LIMBS O F ASTÁNGA-YOGA
1 . I will describe the yoga system (bhakti-misra astdnga-yoga), the object of which is
to concentrate the mind. By foiiowing this the mind attains the joyful spiritual path.
2-4: Yama and Niyama
1.     sva-dharmdcaranam saktyd          execute prescribed duties to the best of ability
2.      vidharmdt ca nivartanam        avoid performing duties not allotted
3.      daivdt labdhena santosa       satisfied with what is achieved by Lord's grace
4.      dtmavit-carandrcanam         worshiping the lotus feet of a spiritual master
5.      grdniya-dharma-nivrttih —» cessation of conventional religious practices
6.      moksa-dharma-ratih        attracted to those which lead to salvation
7.      mita-medhyddanam         eating very frugally
8.      sasvad vivikta-ksema-sevanam         always remaining in secluded place
9.      ahirhsá   non-violence
10.     satyam —>• truthfulness
11.     asteyarh    refraining from theft
12.     yávad-artha-parigrahah       possessing only whatever is necessary
13.     brahmacaryam        celibacy
14.     tapah —>• austerity
15.     saucam     cleanliness
16.     svádhyáyah —>• study of the Vedas
17.     purusa-arcanam       worshiping the Supreme Personality of Godhead
18.     maunam       silence
Note: Bold points in the above list are yamas and rest are niyamas.
5-6: Ásana, Pránáyáma, Pratyáhára, Dháraná, Dhyána and Samádhi
5      Ásana            Attaining steadiness by mastering correct ásanas
Pránáyáma        Controlling the breathing of the vitai air
Pratyáhára       Withdrawing senses from material objects, concentrate mind in the heart
6      Dháraná          Fixing the prána at one spot among the various cakras by mind
Dhyána           Meditating on the limbs of the Lord and Vaikuntha-lilás
Samádhi          The state of the mind's being virtually absorbed in the Lord
7. By these or any other true processes, one must control the contaminated, unbridled
mind and fix it on the Lord.
8. Requirements for Ásana - One should practice breath control by -
(i) Controlling the mind and sitting postures
Canto 3   ^^^^^^^^^//^M
(ii) Establishing a seat in a clean place
(iii) Sitting in svastiká-asana and
(iv) Keeping the body straight
9 - 1 0 : Mind control through Pránáyáma
9. One should purify the passage for prána by the practice of inhaling (püraka),
holding [kumbhaka) and exhaling [recaka), or reversing the process, so that the mind
becomes steady.
10. Very soon the mind of such a yogi becomes pure, just as gold becomes pure tvhen
put into fire and fanned with air.
11. Effects of the practicing of -
•    Pránáyáma           destroys the dosás in the body
•    Dháraná          destroys sins
•        Pratyáhára      frees one from material association with sense objects
•    Dhyána           frees one from the modes (uncontroUable qualities)
12. When the mind becomes steady by the practice of this yoga, looking at the tip of
the nose, one should meditate on the form of the Lord.

3 4 - 3 8 : LIBERATION O F Y G G Í A N D H I S STATE O F M I N D
34. By this meditation, one gradually develops love for the Lord, exhihiting
symptoms like the hairs standing and tears rolling. Gradually, he withdraws his
hook-like mind from all material activity.
Note: Srila Visvanátha Cakravarti Thákura mentions that an unfortunate yogi, in
spite of developing love for the Lord, also withdraws his mind from His beautiful
form, because he is not fixed in that taste. Such a yogi is cheated of bhakti-rasa. The
Lord gives such a yogi the realization of pratyag-dtmá (individual soul), but not
liheration with realization of Paramátmá.

Canto 3 Overview
•
L i n k [3.27.17-20]   [3.28.35-39]: Lord Kapila continues to respond to Devahüti's
question about how the soul is bound and freed.
35. The mind thus detached from material contamination is like the flame of a lamp,
and is actually dovetailed with that of the Supreme Lord, as it is freed from the flow
of material qualities. (Material mind destroyed)
36. Then that detached mind becomes situated in its own glory, transcendental to all
happiness and distress. The yogi realizes the truth of his relationship with the Lord
and discovers that pleasure and pain are actually due to the falsé ego. (Soul devoid of
material coverings)
37. Because he achieved his real identity, he acts as if totally unaware of his body,
like a drunken person.
38. The body of such a liberated yogi is taken charge of by the Lord, and its functions
continue until its destined activities are finished. He does not accept the by-products
of the material body as his own and it appears like a body within a dream.
3 9 - 4 2 : UNDERSTANDING O F T H E Y O G Í
39. Just as a person is different from his son and wealth, though he identifies with
them, the jiva is different from his body. Liberated soul can understand this.
40-41. Just as fire is different from wood, sparks and smoke etc, Bhagaván is also
different from the jiva combined with the senses, the five elements and consciousness.
42. Such a yogi should see the Supersoul in all beings and all beings in the Supersoul.
43. Just as fire appears different in different forms of wood, under different
conditions of the modes, the pure spirit soul manifests itself in different bodies.
44. After conquering insurmountable maya the yogi can be in the self-realized
position.

11-14:    Characteristics of Pure hhakti heyond modes
Note: The qualities of pure bhakti mentioned in 3.25.32 are mentioned here again for
easy understanding.
11-12. Unadulterated devotional service - By hearing about M y qualities mind
flows continuously to Me unobstructed, just as the Ganges flows to the ocean. Such
bhakti is causeless and uninterrupted.
mad-guna-sruti-mdtrena mayi sarva-guhdsaye

L i n k : Kapila explains the lack of material desire in pure devotees, by kaimutya-nydya
(as illustrated by the example of Ganges) - devotees do not accept even spiritual
benedictions, what to speak of accepting material things!
13. Devotees reject liheration without service - M y devotees do not accept the five
kinds of liheration, even when I offer them, unless it involves service to Me.
sdlokya-sdrsti-sdmipya- sdrüpyaikatvam apy uta
dtyamdnarh na grhnanti vind mat-sevanam jandh
L i n k : Then why do they perform bhakti? - because it gives the highest result
14. The highest state of existence is called bhakti, by which one -
(i) surpasses the three gunas (a by-product only) and
(ii) is situated in the transcendental stage iike Me or attain premd directed to Me
sa eva bhakti-yogdkhya dtyantika uddhrtah
yendtivrajya tri-gunam mad-bhdvdyopapadyate
L i n k 11 -> (15-19]: In 3.29.II, Kapila explained that simply by hearing of His name,
quality, etc, one is immediately attracted. That stage is attained by foiiowing the
below mentioned components of bhakti, as confirmed in 3.29.19.
15      i     Executing prescribed duties (svadharma) without material profit
ii    Regularly performing one's devotional activities, without excessive violence
16     iii    Seeing, touching, worshipping, praying and offering obeisance to the deity
iv    Seeing with detachment and with patience, every living entity as spiritual
Canto 3 Overview
17. Qualities of madhyama-adhikán
V      Respecting the spiritual master and the acaryas {mahatarh bahu-mánena)
vi      Showing compassion to the unfortunate (dtnándm anukampayá)
vii     Being friendly to one's equals (maitryá caivdtma-tulyesu)
viii    Executing all activities with regulation and sense control (yamena niyamena)
18    ix      Hearing about spirituál matters (ddhydtmikdnusravandt)
X      Chanting the Lord's boly names (ndma-sankirtaná)
xi      Straight forward behaviour (árjavena)
xii     Associating with saintly persons (drya-sangena)
xiii     Giving up falsé ego (nirahankriyayd)
19-20: Practice of these aiigas of hhakti gives rise to pure hhakti
19. Being thus purified by the above processes (15-18), the mind of such a devotee is
immediately attracted simply by hearing M y name or qualities.
20. Just as air carries an aroma from its source to the nose, bhakti carries one's mind
to the Supreme Lord.
L i n k : In the practice of bhakti, offense creates restriction. Offense generally stems
from disrespect to great devotees. In order to avoid offense to them, one must respect
all living entities. Now, Kapila scolds, out of affection, to benefit and teach His
devotees who do not respect all beings.
21 -27: OvERCOMiNG OBSTACLES IN BHAKTI D U E T O OFFENSES
21-24: Offenses in hhakti due to disregard, hatred and criticism
21-22. Disregard - I am present in the heart of every living being as the Supersoul. If
an ignorant person neglects or disregards (avajhdya) Me in all beings, his worship of
the deity is simply an imitation and is like offering oblations into ashes.
yo mdrh sarvesu bhütesu santam dtmdnam isvaram
hitvárcdm bhajate maudhydd bhasmany eva juhoti sah
23. Hatred - The mind of such a separatist who worships Me hut hates (dvisatah)
other heings does not attain peace.
24. Criticism - I am not pleased with such a person who criticizes other heings
(avamdninah) even if he worships with proper rituals and paraphernalia.
3.29 Explanation Of Devotional Service
aham uccavacair dravyaih kriyayotpannayanaghe
naiva tusye 'rcito 'rcdyám bhüta-grámávamáninah
25-27: Solution - Realize the presence of the Lord in everyone
25. Until one realizes M y presence in his own heart and in the hearts of others, one
should perform his prescrihed duties and worship M y deity.
26. In the form of death, I give fear to those who see differences hetween themselves
and other living entities.
27. Therefore, through charity, respect, friendly hehavior and equal vision, one
should propitiate Me, who ahides in all as their very Self.
L i n k : But having equal vision does not mean that the devotee loses discrimination
based on gradations in the jivas. [One should have discrimination when to behave
friendly and when to give charity, etc.]

1 - 1 8 : LIFE A N D DEATH O F A MATERIALIST
1 . A materialist does not know the power of time hy which he is heing carried
away, just as the clouds do not know the power of wind.
2. Whatever he has acquired with great pain and lahor for his pleasure is destroyed
by time, and he laments for that.
3. H e laments because, under ignorance, he has accepted temporary things related to
this hody like home, land, wealth, etc. as permanent.
4. He finds a particular type of satisfaction i n whatever species he takes hirth in,
and therefore is never averse to such a condition.
5. Bewildered hy maya, he feels little inclined to give up his body, even when in
hell, for he takes delight in hellish enjoyment.
6. Such satisfaction is due to one's deep-rooted attraction for hody, wife, children,
home, animals, wealth and friends, which make him feel quite perfect.

sweet words of his small children.
9. He remains in family life, which is full of diplomacy and politics. If he can
successfuily counteract miseries, he thinks himself happy.

in the lower forms, he becomes purified and is reborn as a humán being.
