44- 46: What is birth and death?
44.      Death - End of the reaction of a jiva's particular activity.
B i r t h - Beginning of a particular type of reaction.
45- 46. Death - When the physical hody, the place where perception of ohjects
occurs, is rendered incapahle of perceiving.
B i r t h - When one hegins to view the physical hody as one's very self.
Example: When the eyeballs (gross matter) are unahle to see objects due to eye
disease, then the eye sense organ (subtle) also cannot see. When both the gross organ
and the subtle organ are incapahle, then the seer becomes incapahle of acting as a
seer.

miserahle, or eager to enjoy in the world.
Do's - Understanding real nature, one should move ahout in the world free
from attachment and steadfast in purpose.
48. Endowed with proper vision, reason, devotional service and detachment^ one
should move ahout in this world made of mdyd without attachment to the hody.

1 - 4 : ATTACHED WORKERS (PRACTITIONERS O F SAKÁMA KARMA)
C A N N O T ATTAIN LIBERATION
1 . A grhamedht (attached worker) performs dharma for enjoying artha and kdma,
and afrer enjoying them; he acts the same way again and again.
2. Such a person is averse to bhakti, but worships the devatás and pitrs with full
faith.
3. As a result, he is elevated to the moon, where he drinks soma juice, and then
returns back to earth.
4. All the planets attained hy such a grhamedht are destroyed when the Lord goes to
His hed of serpents, Ananta Sesa, at the end of the day of Brahmá.

L i n k [22-23] —> 26: In 3.32.22-23, Kapila said that all the results of bhakti, jndna and
yoga are achieved by performing bhakti alone. How is that?
26. The Supreme Personality of Godhead, Bhagavan, is one, but He appears
differently as Brahman, Paramátma or purusa-avatdra, according to the different
processes foUowed by various practitioners.
jhdna-mdtram páram brahma paramdtmesvarah pumdn
drsy-ddibhih prthag bhdvair bhagavdn eka iyate
Note: Because Bhagavan contains both Brahman and Paramátma, the realization of
Bhagaván by bhakti includes the realizations of Paramátma and Brahman. On the
other hand, by practicing jndna for Brahman or yoga for Paramátmá, one does not
attain the position of an associate of the Lord, for this is nowhere stated in scriptures.

32. Either by bhakti (devotional service) or by jndna (philosophical research) one has
to find the same destination, the Supreme Personality of Godhead (Bhagaván).
L i n k : But it is well known that the jndnís attain Brahman and not Bhagavdn!                    Then
how can the destination of both bhaktas and jnanis be the same?
33. Just as a single object is appreciated differently by different senses, the Lord is
one, but according to different scriptural practices He appears to be different.
yathendriyaih prthag-dvdrair  artho bahu-gundsrayah
eko ndneyate tadvad   bhagavdn sdstra-vartmabhih
Note: Perceiving milk with different senses - When we see milk, we see that it is
white; when we taste it, it appears palatable. When we touch milk, it appears cold;
when we smell it, it appears aromatic; and when we hear, we understand that it is
called milk. But one can best appreciate the milk with the tongue, by tasting it and
not with the eyes, nostrils or ears. Similarly...
Approaching the Supreme Lord by different means - Those who try to find the
Lord by mentái speculation may approach His bodily effulgence (impersonal
Brahman); those who try to find Him by mystic yoga may realize Him as the localized
Supersoul; but those who directly approach the Supreme Truth by bhakti-yoga can see
Him face to face as Bhagaván. Thus, the Supreme Lord is the ultimate destination of
all the processes but depending on the particular process one follows, one reaches a
WWm'MWMWmemwmMWMM
Canto 3 Overview   ^HB^'^^íjfíWiÉ^
particular aspect of the Lord. But the Bhagaván realization (by bhakti) includes the
realizations of the Lord's Paramátma and Brahman features as well (3.32.26). Thus
Bhakti is the best path and Bhagaván realization is the ultimate.

bhakti, sakdma and niskdma, varndsrama activities, understanding tbe nature of
dtmd, and firm detachment.

9 - 1 2 : L O R D KAPILA G A V E FINAL INSTRUCTIONS T O D E V A H U T I A N D LEFT
I
9. Kapila, satisfied by the words of His mother, towards whom He was very
affecrionare, replied with gravity.
10. " B y following this easy path of bhakti rhar I have instructed to you, you shall very
soon be liberated, even within your present body.
11. Please have faith in these instructions that are practiced by those who know the
truth, and which will give you fearlessness. Those who are not conversant with these
will attain death."
12. Maitreya: Lord Kapila took permission from his mother and left home.
[ 3 - 3 2 ; DEVAHÚTI ACHIEVED PERFECTION
13. As instructed by Kapila, she engaged in Bhakti in that house of Kardama Muni.
14. Due to intense austerities her body hecame thin and her hair matted.
15-19: Opulence of the palace of Kardama Muni
15. The opulence of the home of Kardama was sometimes envied by higher denizens.
16. There are white bed sheets and mattresses, ivory chairs and benches covered by
cloths of lace with golden filigree, and golden couches which had very soft pillows.
17. There are first-class marble walls which were decorated with valuahle jewels
producing light. The female members were all amply decorated with jewelry.
18. Beautiful gardens, with sweet, fragrant flowers and many fruit bearing trees were
present. The whole atmosphere was as pleasing as possible.
19. The Gandharvas would sing about Kardama Muni's glorious household life.
Kardama gave his wife all protection at all times.
20-21: In spite of opulence, Devahüti lamented for her son
20. Saintly Devahüti, in spite of her many possessions, gave up all such comforts. She
was only sorry that her great son was separated from her.
21. Devahüti's husband had already left home, and then her only son, Kapila, left
home. Although she was pure and knew all the truths, she was very aggrieved at the
loss of her son, just as a cow is affected when her calf dies.
22-23: Devahüti's practice
22. Meditating upon her son, Kapila, the Supreme Lord, she quickly hecame
unattached to her nicely decorated home.
Canto 3 Overview   gl^^gg^J^^Ű^^M^^J^M^j^M
23. Thereafter, having heard with great eagerness and in all detail from her son (in
3.28), Devahüti hegan to meditate constantly upon the form of Visnu.
24-26: Devahüti's Achievement
24-25. She practiced bhakti-yoga endowed with strong renunciation and knowiedge,
and purity of mind. Being fully absorbed in meditation, all misgivings disappeared.
26. Her mind hecame fixed in Bhagaván, and she automatically realized the
knowiedge of Brahman. She attained bliss and all matériái pangs disappeared, as she
was freed from matériái designations.
Note: Devahüti fixed her mind on the full svarüpa of the Lord. If one has complete
knowiedge of Supreme Lord, then the knowiedge of impersonal Brahman and
Paramátma are automatically realized.
27-29: Devahüti's External Disposition
27. Due to attaining eternal trance and freedom from illusion, she forgót her body.
28. She did not become thin as she had no mentái anxiety at that time.
29. Completely absorbed in Vásudeva, she was unaware that her body, with unbound
hair and disarrayed clothing, was being protected by the Lord.
30-32: Devahüti attained perfection
30. By following the principles instructed by Kapila, she hecame liberated and
quickly achieved the Lord, without difficulty.
31. She attained perfection in the sacred and famous place called Siddhapada.
32. The matériái elements of her body melted into water and are now a flowing river,
which is the most sacred of all rivers. Anyone who bathes there also attains
perfection.
3 3 - 3 7 : ACTIVITIES O F L O R D KAPILA
33. Lord Kapila left with the permission of H i s mother and went towards the
northeast (in the direction of Gangá-ságara).
34. He was praised hy the Siddhas, Cáranas, Gandharvas, sages and Apsarás. The
ocean offered H i m oblations and a place of residence (Gaiigá-ságara).
35. Even now Lord Kapila is staying there in trance for the deliverance of the three
worlds, and all the acaryas of the system of Sáhkhya philosophy are worshiping H i m .
36-37. Phala-sruti - This is the purest of all pure discourses. One who hears, reads
or expounds this knowiedge, becomes a devotee and attains the lotus feet and the
abode of the Lord, who is carried by Garuda.

mmxMmmm                                              1. Humanlike Pastimes
1 . HUMANLIKE         PASTIMES
yan martya-lilaupayikam sva-yoga-
máyá-balaih darsayatá grhitam
vismápanam svasya ca saubhagarddheh
paraih padam bhüsana-bhüsanángam
"The Lord appeared (darsayatd grhitam) in the mortal world (martya)
by His internál potency, yoga-máyá [sva-yoga-mdyd-balam).        He came
in   H i s eternal form, which       is just suitable for His pastimes
(lilaupayikath),   which are wonderful (vismdpanam) for everyone, even
for those proud of their own opulence (saubhagarddheh                 páram
padarh), including the Lord Himself in H i s form as the Lord of
Vaikuntha (svasya). Thus H i s [Sri Krsna's] transcendental body is the
ornament of all ornaments (bhüsana-bhüsandngam)."        [SB 3.2.12]
Context: Vidura, being unaware of the departure of Lord Krsna and the Yadu
dynasty from this world, meets Uddhava on the banks of the Yamuná, and asks of
their welfare. I n deep separation from the Lord, Uddhava replies to Vidura's inquiry,
but not directly, instead he speaks a series of verses about Krsna's pastimes and
qualities, inspired by his love or prema.
Emphasis: The above verse in particular emphasizes how the humanlike form of
Lord Krsna is the supreme most, compared to all the other manifestations of the
Supreme Lord. Similarly, attraction and affection towards Lord Krsna is the best,
compared to the other forms. This is the verdict of all acaryas, as it will be evident
from the list of quotations below.
I    Special Verses         *fáí%tlfáfkWif^MW^^
1.   More Mercy: In Vaikuntha, the Lord is merciful toward the liberated living
entities, but in His pastimes in the mortal world. He is merciful even to the fallen
souls. (3.2.12 P)
2.   Excellent Pastimes: The rása-lllá and householder life with sixteen thousand
wives is wonderful even for Náráyana in Vaikuntha what more for other living
entities. (3.2.12 P)
3.   Faultless: One should not think that the Lord has somé fault because He has
humán pastimes. On the contrary, the humán pastimes are superior to the
Vaikuntha pastimes. (3.2.12 V )
4.   Identical: Though it is said that the Lord accepted this form, His form is
inseparable from Himself. (3.2.12 V )
5.   Unique Sweetness: The sweetness and intimacy Lord Krsna manifested in
Vrndávana constitute a unique kind of opulence found nowhere else, even in
Vaikuntha. (10.90.47 P)
6.   Manifestation of Yogamáyá: "Even a fractional understanding of Krsna's
heauty can mérge all three worlds in the ocean of love. He attracts all living
entities within the three worlds. The transcendental form of Krsna is shown to
the world by Lord Krsna's internál, spiritual energy, which is a transformation of
pure goodness." ( C C Madhya-lila 21.99-103)
7.   64th Quality: Srila Rüpa Gosvami quotes this verse (3.2.12) as evidence to the
64* quality of Lord Krsna, rupa-madhuryam,      that is, the sweetness of His form.
(BRS 2.1.215)
8.   Topmost Rasa: While describing about the various kinds of rasas or tastes that
one can experience in life (from the mundane self-centered happiness or matériái
rasa, right up to the rasa for the Supreme Lord), Srila Jiva Gosvami quotes this
verse (3.2.12) to conclude that the rasas of perceiving Lord Krsna, is the topmost.
(Priti Sandarhha, Anuccheda 110)
9.   Beautifies Ornaments: The uddipanas (impetuses for ecstatic love), such as the
ornaments and paraphernalia of the Lord, become empowered and glorious due
to contact with the Lord. Quoting "bhüsana-bhüsanángam,"         Srila Jiva Gosvami
proves this. (Priti Sandarhha, Anuccheda 155)
á*íi                  1. Humanlike Pastimes
•5
10. Beautifies Qualities: In the discussion of the 64 qualities of Lord Krsna, Srila
Jiva Gosvami and Srila Visvanátha Cakravarti Thakura, both quote this verse in
their commentaries, to show how these 64 qualities become even more amazing
when exhibited by Krsna, especially when He becomes absorbed in the role of an
ordinary humán on earth. Lurther, Srila Jiva Gosvami states that these 64
qualities are not the cause of amazement for those who relish Krsna, since they
are absorbed in His sweetness. (Commentaries on BRS 2.1.39-40)
11. Supreme Personality of Godhead: This verse (along with other verses from the
Bhágavatam and Bhagavad-gitá), clearly shows that Lord Krsna is the Originál
Personality of Godhead (Srila Jiva Gosvámi's Sarva-samvádini commentary of
Krsna Sandarhha, Anuccheda 82)
12. Bestows Great Happiness: Lord Krsna exists with all superior qualities. One of
them is His quality of "bestowing great happiness." 3.2.12 is an example of this
quality. (Jiva Gosvámi's Durgama Sangamani tika of BRS 1.1.1)
13. Rare But Unique: " T h e attraction for Krsna is highly uncommon yet most
blissful even in comparison to the other avatáras of the L o r d . " As evidence for
this, Srila Jiva Gosvámi and Srila Visvanátha Cakravarti, both quote 3.2.12, in
their commentaries of BRS 2.5.108.
14. Pleasure to Other Forms: The other forms of the Lord, upon seeing Krsna
become fiiled with bliss. (1.1.3 V )
15. Appeared to give Joy: The Lord appeared in this world not only on the request
of lord Brahmá, but also appeared to give joy to His devotees by revealing His
inconceivahle, variegated form, qualities, and pastimes fiiled with rasa, since He
is the Lord of yoga-máyá. (11.6.42 V )
16. Attractive: "Lveryone showed more affection to Lord Gauranga than to their
own    sons,   for   the   Lord    stole   everyone's    heart   just by His    presence."
Bhaktisiddhánta Sarasvati Tháküra comments on this statement that "the Lord of
the samvit potency is so powerful that He forcibly attracts all transcendentally
pure objects." And refers to verse 3.2.12 as an evidence. ( C B Adi-khanda 4.106)

2 . EXTRAORDINARY M E R C Y
aho baki yam stana-kála-kütam
jighámsayápáyayad apy asádhvi
lebhe gatirii dhátry-ucitám tato 'nyam
karii vá dayálum saranam vrajema
"Alas (aho), how shall I take shelter (saranam vrajema) of one
more merciful than He (tato 'nyam kam vá dayálurh) who granted
the position (lebhe gatim) of mother (dhátry-ucitám)          to a she-
demon [Pütaná] (baki) although she was unfaithful (asádhvi) and
she prepared (ápáyayad)       deadly poison to be sucked from her
breast (stana-kála-kütam)}"    [SB 3.2.23]
Context: Continuing his reply to Vidura, Uddhava, remembers the Lord's sweet
mercy during His childhood pastimes. He now quotes an extrémé incidence of the
Lord's exhihition of compassion, not unto a devotee, but instead unto a démon who
came in disguise, harboring the desire to kill H i m .
This amazing display of mercy by the Lord increasingly spreads the glories of His
fame    and character.     The following list     of     citations   showcases the various
appreciations of the acaryas about this transcendental pastime and nature of the
Supreme Lord.
1.   Extrémé Mercy: "Here is an example of the extrémé mercy of the Lord, even to
His enemy. It is said that a noble man accepts the good qualities of a person of
doubtful character, just as one accepts nectar from a stock of poison." (3.2.23 P)
2. Extraordinary Mercy
2.   Ultimate Shelter: " T h e Lord accepts the least qualification of the living entity
and awards him the highest reward. That is the standard of His character.
Therefore, who but the Lord can be the ultimate shelter?" (3.2.23 P)
3.   Special E x a m p l e : "Though Pütaná only had the dress of a devotee, she attained
love suitable to a devotee. Though she hated the Lord, He made her a special
example. This incident shows Krsna's quality of giving liberation and bhakti to
even those who hate h i m . " (3.2.23 V )
4.   Gratitude: I n explaining the process of devotional service to Srila Sanátana
Goswámi, Lord Caitanya quotes the aho baki yam verse to suhstantiate how the
Lord fulfills all desires, and even gives Himself to His devotees who have given up
all other engagements to sérve H i m . ( C C Madhya lila 22.96-98)
5.   Source    of Compassion:        Srila   Bhaktisiddhánta   Sarasvati tháküra   in his
commentary to Sri Caitanya Bhágavata writes how the verse "tomd' vine saranya
dayálu náhi ára" (there is no shelter or source of compassion other than You) is
explained by the aho baki yam verse. ( C B Ádi-khanda 13.168)
6.   Himalayan Blunder: "Only the most unfortunate, unintelligent, foolish, hellish
person gives up the service to the lotus feet of Krsna, which is the topmost
occupational duty, and contemplates or endeavors for anything else." Srila
Bhaktisiddhánta Sarasvati tháküra quotes the aho baki yam verse, to expound his
statement. (CB Madhya-khanda 1.160)
7.   Guna-Gráhí: Srila Bhaktisiddhánta Sarasvati tháküra quotes the aho baki yam
verse as evidence to how "the Lord only accepts the good qualities of the fault-
filled living entities; He is known as guna-gráhi.    He who sees only the good in
others. He does not find any fault in others." ( C B Antya-khanda 1.276)
8.   Perfection of Gokula-nandana: Rüpa Gosvámi states, " I n Dváraká, Mathurá
and other places, Krsna appears as perfect and more perfect. But in Gokula,
Krsna appears as the most perfect." Jiva Gosvámi comments on how Lord Krsna
in Gokula is perfect in majesty, sweetness and mercy by quoting appropriate
verses. Lor fullness in mercy, he quotes the aho baki yam verse. (Durgama
Sangamani tika on BRS 2.1.223)

3 . PERSONALISED TRANSFORMATION
Wifp
tasyáravinda-nayanasya padáravinda-
kinjalka-misra-tulasí-makaranda-váyuh
antar-gatah sva-vivarena cakára tesám
sahksobham aksara-jusám api citta-tanvoh
"When the breeze [vdyuh) carrying the aroma [makaranda) of tulasi leaves
from the toes [kinjalka-misra-tulasi)     of the lotus feet (padáravinda)         of the
lotus eyed Personality of Godhead (tasya áravinda-nayanasya)          entered [antar-
gatah] the nostrils [sva-vivarena]      of those sages [Kumdras]       [tesám],     they
experienced (cakára) a change (sanksobham) both in body and in mind (citta-
tanvoh), even though (api) they were attacbed to the impersonal Brahman
understanding (aksara-jusám)."    [3.15.43]
Context: This verse describes the blossoming of sánta-rati         (neutral appreciation) in
the four Kumáras when they smelled the fragrance of the lotus feet of the Lord
mixed with the aroma of tulasi. Previously having bhakti ringed with jndna, they now
acquired pure devotion for the Lord. The experience of the four Kumáras stands as
tacit proof of the extraordinary attractive power of the Supreme Lord's form and
qualities, and thus proves the superior realization and hliss of the devotees (bhaktas)
in comparison to all the other types of transcendentalists. The following are somé
interesting insights on this verse from the Vaisnava    ácáryas.
3. Personalised Transformation
1.   Being a Servant over an Equal: "Because of the fragrance of His lotus feet,
carried by the air and mixed with the aroma of tulasi, their minds changed;
instead of becoming one with the Supreme Lord, they thought it wise to be
devotees. Becoming a servitor of the lotus feet of the Lord is better than becoming
one with the L o r d . " (3.15.43 P)
2.   Brahmavadis vs. Mayavadis: "Those who are actually on the platform of
Brahman realization and who have not offended the lotus feet of Krsna can
immediately become Vaisnavas simply by smelling the aroma of the Lord's lotus
feet. But those who are offenders or demons are never attracted to the Lord's
personal feature, even though they may visit the Lord's temple many times." ( C C
Madhya lila 17.142 P)
3.   61 expianations of the átmáráma verse: 3.15.43 is quoted twice in this section
by Lord Caitanya, stating how the Kumaras hecame devotees ( C C Madhya lila
24.45 and 115)
4.   Átmárámas attracted: This is one of the verses Lord Caitanya speaks to
Prakasánanda Sarasvati to show how even átmárámas are attracted to Lord
Krsna. ( C C Madhya lila 25.158)
5.   Lord's M e r c y : Lven the most prominent jnanis who have realized oneness after
gaining knowiedge of átmá and brahman enter pure bhakti only by the mercy of
the Lord. (Jiva Cosvámi's commentary on BRS 1.2.20-21)
6.   Ecstatic symptoms: Lven those persons expert in impersonal brahma-jnána        later
developed ecstatic bodily symptoms from experiencing the form of the Lord. (Jiva
Coswami's commentary on BRS 2.1.293)
7.   T u l a s i as stimuli: Rupa Cosvami quotes 3.15.43 as evidence to how fragrance of
tulasi (among other items like conch, forest, river, etc.) can be stimuli for both
ddsa-bhaktas as well as the sdnta-bhaktas. (BRS 3.1.21-23)
8.   Lord vs. Brahman:
•    Superiority of seeing the Lord over realizing Brahman is made clear by this verse,
3.15.43 (Priti sandarhha anuccheda 7)
•    " A l l the happiness of impersonal brahman is as insignificant as the water in the
hoof print of a cow." (BRS 1.1.39) Llaborating on this point in his Durgama
sangamani tika, Jiva Cosvámi quotes SB 3.15.43, as a comparison of realization
of bliss of the Supreme Lord and Brahman
I        Special Verses   ^^BtíMÍ)í'^§ir«AÍ^^^^
9.   Realization of Sweetness: Sweetness is the pleasure experienced through the
Lord's form, qualities and pastimes. By realizing sweetness one attains love for
the Lord. Realizing only brahman produces only bliss for the self, which is
covered by the realization of the sweetness, which happened in the case of the
Kumáras on seeing the Lord. (Jiva Gosvami's Durgama sangamani tika on BRS
4.4.15)
10. Case Study: ' A jnání who had previously achieved complete perfection through
philosophical and realized knowiedge recognizes the Lord's lotus feet to be the
supreme.' Kumaras are an evidence for this as indicated by 3.15.43. (11.19.3 V )
11. Adbhuta-rasa: One of the causes of adbhuta-rasa (amazement) is 'experiencing
the bliss of the Lord's sweetness as greater than the bliss of impersonal brahman.'
This verse (3.15.43) is an evidence for this. (Durgama sangamani tika of BRS
4.8.2)
4. Association Of Devotees
satath prasangan mama vírya-saihvido
bhavanti hrt-karna-rasáyanáh   katháh
taj-josanad asv apavarga-vartmani
sraddhá ratir bhaktir anukramisyati
" I n the association of pure devotees (satath prasangan), discussion
(sarhvidah) of the pastimes (katháh)     and activities of the Supreme
Personality of Godhead (mama vtrya) is very pleasing and satisfying
(rasdyandh)     to the ear and the heart (hrt-karna).      By cultivating
(josandt)     such    knowiedge    (tat) one gradually becomes (dsu)
advanced on the path of liberation           (apavarga-vartmani),      and
thereafter he is freed (sraddhd),     and his attraction becomes fixed
(ratir).    Then real devotion and devotional service begin         (bhaktir
anukramisyati)."       [3.25.25]
Context: Lord Kapila in H i s instructions to Mother           Devahüti,     explains how
attachment for a devotee is beneficial, as it can lead one towards liberation. The Lord
then goes on to explain who exactly is a devotee by stating the qualities of a sádhu.
Linally, he quotes this verse, linking the association of devotees with the development
of bhakti.
Emphasis: This verse highlights the importance of devotee association, as well as the
gradual development of bhakti.
jÜl^l    Special Verse   J|J||r||^|^^|^^^
Srila Prabhupáda liberally quotes this verse in many purports to highiight the
importance of devotee association. He also provides vitai lessons that express how
this verse can be better appreciated. The following is a compilation of his words
along with one or two quotes from other ácáryas on the same note.
With the Association of Devotees...
1.   transcendental suhject matter becomes clear (BG 17.26-27 P)
2.   and by contemplating on the transcendental activities of the Lord, the path to
liberation is opened, and one is freed (3.25.25 P)
3.   one associates with the Lord (3.25.25 P)
4.   one develops the consciousness for rendering service to the Lord (3.25.25 P)
5. only, can the words of Lord Krsna be fully potent and relishable to the heart and
ear. (4.9.11 P, 7.8.1 P)
6.   the heart of a materialistic person is cleansed of lusty desires, just as silver
becomes shiny by being polished (4.22.20)
7.   one is awakened (from dreaming/sleep) and immediately the spirit soul is situated
in his own glory by understanding his constitutional position as the Lord's eternal
servant. (4.22.20)
8.   one can understand the transcendental name, fame, quality and activities of the
Supreme Personality of Godhead. (4.24.59 P)
9.   one becomes attacbed to hearing and chanting about the Lord. Thus cultivating
Krsna consciousness, one can become faitbful to the Lord, devoted to the Lord
and attacbed to the Lord, and thus one can very quickly attain full Krsna
consciousness. (4.29.39-40)
10. one can relish the glorification of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Just
through a little association with a pure devotee, one can become successful in his
journey back to Godhead (5.13.21 P)
11. one can authoritatively understand the position of the Lord and His devotees.
(7.4.44 P)
12. even a démon can become a devotee (8.22.36 P)
13. the glorification of the Lord becomes potent (4.20.24 P)
14. and their instructions, one becomes more and more purified of matériái desires
(10.10.17 P)
15. the mind develops attraction to the Lord (3.25.25 V )
16. fortunate     souls attain faith in unalloyed devotional service (Bhaktivinoda
tháküra's Amnáya-sütra, Part two, Chapter two, Sutra 56)

3.   one cannot understand that the Absolute Truth can be a person and have personal
activities. (3.25.25 P)
4.   one will think that these activities are fictional, manufactured stories because
spiritual life is not explained to them in the proper mood. (3.25.25 P)

(BRS 1.3.12)
5.   "One must strive to achieve the association of pure devotees," Bhaktivinoda

statement by quoting 3.25.25 (Vaisnava tosani 10.14.5).

5. SERVICE SURPASSES LIBERATION
salokya-sarsti-samipya- sarupyaikatvam apy uta
diyamánaih na grhnanti viná mat-sevanam janáh
" A pure devotee (janáh) does not accept (na grhnanti)      any kind of
liberation—sálokya, sársti, sámipya, sárüpya or (api) ekatva—even if
(uta) they are offered (diyamánarh),    without service for Me      (viná
mat-sevanarh)." [3.29.13]
Context: This verse appears in Kapiladeva's explanation of mixed (3.29.8-10), and
pure devotional service (3.28.11-13). Sálokya means living on the same planet as the
Lord. Sársti means having powers similar to the Lord's. Sámipya means staying near
the Lord. Sárüpya        means having a form similar to the Lord's. Ekatvam           means
merging with the Lord. Devotees do not accept these five kinds of liberation if they
don't offer them a chance to rendet service to the Lord. The following are the various
facets of this verse that are highlighted by the Vaisnava ácáryas.
1.   Lord's Service as Priority
(i) The yogis and empiric philosophers desire cessation of the process of birth
and death, but a devotee is satisfied to remain even in this matériái world
and execute devotional service. (3.29.13 P)
(ii) Por a devotee there is no question of desiring any matériái or spiritual
prosperity. He simply aspires to sérve the Lord. That is his highest
happiness. (3.29.13 P)
(iii) Devotees do not accept these without service to me. This means that if somé
devotees do accept these things, they accept for service to me. (3.29.13 V )
wmMtmm.                                               5. Service Surpasses Liberation
(iv)    Sáyujya by its nature is without service since there is only oneness in it.
Sárüpya is useful for service, with a form of great beauty. Thus devotees
accept these things according to how useful they are in their service. (Priti
Sandarhha 51)
2.   Kaimutya-nyáya: Kapila explains the lack of matériái desire in the devotees who
have pure bhakti, by the method of kaimutya-nyáya.         Devotees do not accept even
spiritual henedictions, what to speak of accepting matériái things! (3.29.13 V )
3.   Lord's Test: By showing or promising [diyamánam)             enjoyment and powers to
devotees, Lord tests H i s devotees, who were filled with devotion. (1.7.40 V )
4.   T w o kinds of desires: one is desire for happiness and power; the other, is desire
for prema. Devotees never accept the first variety. 3.29.13 is an evidence for this.
(BRS 1.2.56-57)
5.   B h a k t i Superior to Liberation
(i)    Rüpa Gosvámi quotes this verse as proof of how bhakti is superior to
liberation. (BRS 1.2.28)
(ii) Jiva Gosvami quotes this verse in his commentary, to show how devotees
who are like makaras (sharks) frolic in the ocean of bhakti-rasa, ignoring
the rivers of liberation (Durgama sangamani tiká of BRS 1.1.4)
(iii)   Haridás tháküra quotes this verse to deride the desire for liberation ( C C
Antya 3.189)
6.   G o a l of L i f e : This verse (among other verses) is quoted in order to state that
liberation as a humán goal is useless. (Priti Sandarhha 21)
7.   Similar Verse: "Lven though dharma, artha, káma               and moksa automatically
come to the devotees, they do not look at them or accept them." Bhaktisiddhánta
Sarasvati tháküra quotes 3.29.13 as a reference in his commentary. ( C B Antya-
khanda 5.059)

6 . WORSHIPABLE CHANTER
yan-námadheya-sravanánukírtanád
yat-prahvanád yat-smaranád api kvacit
svádo 'pi sadyah savanaya kalpate
kutah punas te bhagavan nu darsanat
" T o say nothing {kutah nu) of the spiritual advancement of persons who see the
Supreme Person (bhagavan) face to face (darsanat), even a person born in a family of
dog-eaters (svádo 'pi) immediately (sadyah) becomes eligible (kalpate) to perform
Vedic sacrifices (savanáya) if he once (kvacit) utters the holy name of the Supreme
Personality of Godhead (yat námadheya) or chants about H i m (aniikirtanát), hears
about His pastimes (sravana), offers H i m obeisances (yat prahvanát)       or even
remembers H i m (yat smaranát)." [3.33.6]
aho bata sva-paco 'to garíyán
yaj-jihvágre vartate náma tubhyam
tepus tapas te juhuvuh sasnur áryá
brahmánücur náma grnanti ye te
" O h , how glorious (aho bata) are they whose tongues (yaj-jihvágre)    are chanting
Your holy name! (náma tubhyam)        Even if born in the families of dog-eaters (sva-
pacah), such persons are worshipable (gariyán).    Persons (ye) who chant the holy
name of Your Lordship (te náma grnanti), must have executed all kinds of austerities
(tepus tapah) and fire sacrifices (juhuvuh) and achieved all the good manners of the
Áryans (áryáh). T o be chanting the holy name of Your Lordship, they must have
bathed at holy places of pilgrimage (sasnuh), studied the Vedas (brahma anücuh) and
fulfilled everything required." [3.33.7]
&'Íf'Q^éí'^l^éf'&ét'^l^éf'G^                                                          6. Worshipable Chante
Context: Kapila gives His final instructions to mother Devahüti and in response, she
offers a set of prayers, imbued with her realizations (3.33.2-8). Verses 6 and 7,
presents the glories of the holy name. A devotee who chants the holy name, is said to
have attained all the necessary qualifications to be engaged in Vedic sacrifices, study
etc. Since the holy name of Lord Krsna is Krsna Himself, it is completely spiritual and
transcendental. Thus chanting of the holy name somehow or the other is always
effective. However, offenseless chanting is emphasized by Srila Prabhupáda. The
following are various quotations of ácáryas in relation to these verses.
1.    Potency of H o l y Name: Heréin the spiritual potency of chanting, hearing or
remembering the holy name of the Supreme Lord is greatly stressed. (3.33.6 P)
2.    Offenseless Chanting:
i.   Sridhara Svámi's remark, anena püjyatvam       laksyate (by this, he is seen as
respectable), is applicable if one chants offenselessly, for he immediately
becomes more than a bráhmana. (3.33.6 P)
ii.    As it is stated in 3.33.6, a person who has once offenselessly chanted the holy
name becomes immediately eligible to do Vedic sacrifices. (3.33.7 P)
3.    Vanquish sinful reactions:
i.   Devotional service vanquishes the reactions of sinful - pápám, kütam, bijam
and phalonmukham - as mentioned in Padma Purána. (6.1.15 P)
ii.   Srila Rüpa Goswámi quotes 3.33.6 to show how chanting leads to the
destruction of manifest sinful reactions, prdrabdha. (BRS 1.1.21)
iii.   Srila Jiva Goswámi and Srila Bhaktivinoda Tháküra writes that 3.33.6
highlights how bhakti easily destroys the prdrabdha sins that result in taking
birth in a low-class family. (Bhakti Sandarhha Anuccheda 128, Bhakti Tattva
Viveka Chapter I I I )
4.    Mistake of vaisnave játi-buddhih:
i.   "Those who find fault in the Western Vaisnavas should consider this
statement from Srimad-Bhágavatam and the commentary on this verse by
Srila Jiva Gosvámi." ( C C Madhya 16.186 P)
ii.   C B Madhya 10.100 reads, " A l l the scriptures declare that a Vaisnava may be
born in any family, but he is certainly situated in the topmost position." Srila
Bhaktisiddhánta Sarasvati Tháküra quotes verses 3.33.6-7 in his commentary
as evidence.
iii.        Lord Caitanya quotes 3.33.7 to glorify Haridas Thakura ( C C Madhya
11.192)
iv.         Vallabha Bhatta glorifies Rüpa Goswámi and Sri Vallabha's position as the
most exalted due to chanting constantly. ( C C Madhya 19.72)
V.         Kálidása quotes 3.33.7 in glorification of Jhadu Tháküra ( C C Antya 16.27)
vi.         Mahá-pátra glorifies the humility of the Muslim governor, who become
transformed simply by seeing the Lord by quoting 3.33.6. ( C C Madhya
16.168)
vii.        The devotee should not be disrespected even if he comes from low caste or
has had conduct (like eating dogs). (Bhakti Sandarhha Anuccheda 247)
5. Asamskrta: A person who is not purified by the prescribed process of samskara is
called asamskrta.        Chanting of holy name qualifies a asarhskrta to become a
bráhmana. ( C C Antya 3.124 P)
6.    Purpose of Vedas: One must be intelligent enough to understand the purpose of
the Vedas, without being attacbed to the rituals only. ( B G 2.46 P)
7.    Beyond Vedic Study:
i.    Advanced yogis are not attracted to the rituals of the scriptures, but they
automatically become attracted to the yoga principles, which can elevate
them to complete Krsna consciousness, the highest yoga perfection. (BG
6.44 P)
ii.       One who has taken shelter of the Lord's holy name, which is identical with
the Lord, does not have to study Vedánta philosophy, for he has already
completed all such study. ( C C Adi 7.72 P)
iii.       Bhaktisiddhánta Sarasvati Tháküra quotes 3.33.7 (among other verses) as an
answer to the question " W h y did Lord Caitanya leave the study of Vedánta
to make a big show of kirtana?" (CB Antya-khanda 8.134)
8.    But not disregard Vedic study: Taking advantage of these verses, there are somé
sahajiyás who, taking everything very cheaply, consider themselves elevated
Vaisnavas but do not care even to touch the Vedánta philosophy. A real Vaisnava
should, however, study Vedánta philosophy, but if after studying Vedánta one
does not adopt the chanting of the Lord's holy name, he is no better than a
Máyávádi. ( C C Adi 7.72 P)
6. Worshipable Chanter
9. T i p of Tongue: It is said that those who simply chant the holy name with the tip
of the tongue are glorious. One does not even have to chant the holy name and
understand the whole procedure, namely the offensive stage, offenseless stage and
pure stage. (3.33.7 P)
10. One Name: It is said hetein that náma, a singular number, one name, Krsna or
Ráma, is sufficient. It is not that one has to chant all the holy names of the Lord.
(3.33.7 P)
11. If once, why not always?: If one chants once only, it is to be understood that he
has already passed all the examinations, not to speak of those who are chanting
always, twenty-four hours a day. (3.33.7 P)
12. Unto Y o u only: One must chant God's name (tubhyam), not, as the Máyávádi
philosophers say, any name, such as a demigod's name or the names of God's
energies. (3.33.7 P)
13. Chant Pleasingly: The holy name has to be chanted to please the Supreme Lord,
and not for any sense gratification or professional purpose. If this pure mentality
is there, then even though a person is born of a low family, such as a dog-eater's,
he is so glorious that not only has he purified himself, but he is quite competent
to deliver others. (3.33.7 P)
14. Windfall: By the grace of Lord Caitanya, one who chants the             mahá-mantra
attains the highest perfectional stage, which was attained by Pracetás. (A.IAAA P)
15. B i r t h rights are wrong: One need not have taken birth in an aristocratic
bráhmana family to attain the lotus feet of Krsna, nor is a person born in a low
family unfit for achieving Krsna's lotus feet. ( C C Antya 4.70 P)
16. Vaisnava is already a Bráhmana: "Though a Vaisnava appears in a dog-eater
family, still he is not a non-bráhmana.        His brahminical qualifications are
eternally perfected or previously acquired." (Bhaktisiddhánta Sarasvati Tháküra
in Bráhmana and Vaisnava, Appendixes: Viprasámya, "Brahmanism is their pre-
attained right.")
7. TIMELINE O F EMBRYONIC DEVELOPMENT
Embryology means the study of the growth of the embryo, from the fertilization of
an ovum to the birth of a child. Ancient Vedic texts like           Srimad-Bhágavatam,
Mahábhárata and the Purdnas also give this knowiedge. I n 3.31, Lord Kapila explains
how the embryo develops in the womh. The following table presents embryology
described by Lord Kapila along with the understanding of the modern medical
science that has been developed in over the past few decades. This comparison reveals
the richness of the Bhágavatam in presenting all the Information relevant for the
humán being.

medical science presented in column 4.

f a ^ F i v E         LAYERS O F IGNORANCE
w
sasarjágre 'ndha-támisram atha támisram ádi-krt
mahámoham ca mohám ca tamás cájnána-vrttayah
"Brahmá fitst created {sasarja agre) the nescient engagements [ajnána
vrttayah) like self-deception (tamah), the sense of death (andha-támisram),
anger after frustration {támisram), the sense of falsé ownership (mahá-
moham), and the illusory bodily conception, or forgetfulness of one's real
identity (mohám)." [3.12.2]
"Before the factual creation of the living entities in different varieties of species, the
conditions under which a living being in the matériái world has to live were created
by Brahmá." (3.12.2 P) What are these conditions? "The first condition of matériái
existence created is forgetfulness of one's real identity." (3.12.2 P) Why is this so?
"Unless a living entity forgets his real identity, it is impossible for him to live in the
matériái conditions of life." (3.12.2 P) Srila Prabhupáda gives the relation between
these five ajnána-vrttis, in his purport to this verse. And Srila Visvanátha Cakravarti
Tháküra's commentary on 3.12.2, co-relates these terms with Patanjali's Yogasütras,
describing five kinds of klesas (sufferings) as given below.

It's interesting to note, that this comparison, provides another kind of definition
altogether. Since the elements of ajnána and klesa match, based on the law of
transitivity, it is implicit that ajnána, ignorance is equal to klesa, suffering. Therefore,
ignorance has been directly equated with suffering.

A                          1. Glories of Bhakti Yoga
. GLORIES O F BHAKTI Y O G A
Srimad-Bhágavatam      repeatedly emphasizes that bhakti-yoga is the best path that
reconnects the conditioned living entity to the Supreme Lord Krsna. The supremacy
of bhakti-yoga over other paths such as astánga-yoga           and jhána-yoga     in uplifting the
soul is repeatedly established in the Bhágavatam.             The following is a list of select
verses from the third canto that glorify the path of bhakti, categorized into relevant
sections. Only the themes of the verses are given so that the readers may appreciate
them at a glancé.

the Lord awards them His own abode.

2 . GLORIES O F KRSNA-KATHÁ
The glorification of the transcendental pastimes and activities of the Lord are found
throughout all the cantos of Srimad-Bhágavatam. The magnificent effects of hearing
and speaking Krsna kathá are repeatedly reminded to the reader of the Bhágavatam
through the realizations of various devotees conversing on the topics of Krsna in the
Bhágavatam. Because the glories of Krsna-kathá are unlimited, there can't be any
limit to the number of times they appear throughout this glorious text. The following
is a list of jewel-like verses, duly captioned, from the third canto, glorifying Krsna-
kathá. The context and elaborate understanding of these verses may be obtained
from the originál text.

The lotus feet of the Supreme Lord are always adored by H i s loving devotees. Since
the devotees apply all their energy and their activities in the service of the Lord's lotus
feet, there must be somé transcendental pleasure in them. Sometimes the Supreme
Personality of Godhead Himself wonders how much transcendental pleasure is
within Himself, so much so that He licks His own toe just to taste the nectar for
which the devotees always hankering. The following are somé beautiful verses from
the Third Canto that reveal various glories of the Lord's lotus feet and inspite us to
take shelter of them with more zeal.
BEAUTY
1.   Adorned with the marks of a thunderbolt, a goad, a banner and a lotus.
Splendor of ruby naiis
2. Moonlike toenails and fingernails appear like the petals of a flower.
[3.8.26 puthsárh     sva-kámáya...]
3.   Resembles the orb of the moon and dispels the thick gloom of one's heart.
[3.28.21 sancintayed bhagavatas       caranáravindam...]
FuLFiLLER O F A L L DESIRES
4.   Gives anything within the realm of dharma, artha, káma and moksa. [3.4.15]
5. True vessél to cross samsára    [bhava-sindhu-potam].   Lven bestows matériái desires
for who pray for them. [3.21.14 ye máyayá te hata-medhasas       tvat...]
6. The Lord's lotus feet are the source of everything. [3.21.15]

Srimad-Bhagavatam is a coUection of conversations amongst pure hearted devotees
like - Sukadeva Goswámi and Pariksit Mahárája, Süta Gosvámi and Saunakádi Rsis,
Maitreya and Vidura, etc. Along with the contents of these conversations, it's a great
learning experience to look at the wonderful mood and attitűdé of the speakers and
hearers, and the consciousness with which they discuss krsna-kathá. A speaker of
Srimad-Bhágavatam     must be authoritative and competent in conveying its real
meaning, having personally realized the subject matter. And to receive this Vedic
knowiedge, one must approach a spiritual master with submission, inquire in a
humhle mood and hear the expianations attentively. Following are somé examples
from the Third Canto that inspite us in out hearing and speaking of krsna-kathá.
iDEAL SPEAKERS
Uddhava
• Attacbed to the Lord - Upon being questioned, he could initially not speak due
to the ecstasy in remembering Lord Krsna. (3.2.1)
• Great devotee - Constant companion of Lord Krsna (3.1.25), assimilated love of
God. (3.2.5)
• Learned - Great student of Brhaspati. (3.1.25)
• Authoritative - Received knowiedge from the Supreme Lord. (3.4.20)
• Surrendered - FoUowed Krsna's instructions to go to Badarikásrama. (3.4.21)
• Etiquette - Requested Vidura to take instruction from Maitreya instead of
himself, being junior to Maitreya (maryádá-vyatikrama). (3.4.26)
Maitreya
• Learned - He was a sage of unfathomed knowiedge (3.5.1)

An important theme in the Srimad-Bhágavatam concerns the feelings of sadness
expressed by the devotees when their beloved Lord is not present. Actually such
feelings of separation are completely transcendental. The devotees constantly relish
qualities. Because He is Absolute, there is no difference between meeting H i m
directly and remembering H i m in separation.
The third canto presents wonderful examples of these moods in the characters of
Uddhava, Vidura, Devahüti and the two gatekeepers of Vaikuntha. Uddhava and
Vidura were both intimate associates of Krsna during His pastimes at the end of
Dvapara-yuga, and they both had to experience Krsna's sad departure (3.1-3). I n
these chapters Uddhava's feelings of love are beautifully described and Vidura's love
leads him to seek knowiedge about Krsna's pastimes from Maitreya Rsi. Jaya and
Vijaya experienced separation from the Lord, by being cursed to take three births as
demons. Devahüti had to suffer the absence of her son. Lord Kapila, who left home
after teaching her in transcendental science.
1.   Overwhelmed by Vidura's questions: Uddhava faints upon hearing Vidura's
inquiries about Krsna. (3.1.25 V )
2.   Unable to speak: Uddhava could not answer Vidura immediately due to the
intense pain upon remembering his dear Lord. (3.2.1)
3.   Motionless and dead silent: Going deeper and deeper into the ecstasy of love in
separation, while remembering the Lord's lotus feet, Uddhava remained blissful
and motionless. (3.2.4)
4.   Ecstatic symptoms: Goosebumps covered his entire body and tears of separation
flowed from his closed eyes and he completely assimilated extensive love for the
Lord. (3.2.5)
7. Feelings of Separation from the Lord
5. Remembered the gopis who were so stunned in separation of Krsna that they
couldn't perform their daily duties. (3.2.14)
6.   Narration of pastimes: T o mitigate their distresses of separation, Uddhava
recoliects many of Krsna's pastimes. (3.2-3)
7.   H e follows K r s n a although knowing H i s contrary desire: Despite knowing
Krsna's plan to destroy the Yadus, Uddhava could not leave H i m . (3.4.5)
8.   Heart pains in separation: Especially because the Lord had taught Uddhava in
self-knowledge, his heart now pained due to separation from H i m . (3.4.20)
9.   Service mitigates Separation: Uddhava was eager to proceed to Badarikásrama
and execute the instructions given to him by Krsna. (3.4.21)
10. News of Krsna's disappearance is unbearable: Vidura already knew about
Krsna's departure, but out of anguish he asks Uddhava again. (3.1.25 P)
11. Krsna-katha and K r s n a are the same: Vidura wants to remember Krsna through
Maitreya's expianations. (3.5.13 P)
12. Overwhelmed: Understanding that he was remembered by Lord Krsna [while
departing from this world], Vidura began to cry loudly, overwhelmed by the
ecstasy of love. (3.4.35)
JAYA A N D V I J A Y A
13. M a y we not forget the L o r d : Upon being cursed by the four Kumáras, the two
doorkeepers accepted their punishment, but expressed a deep desire to not forget
the Lord in their absence from Vaikuntha. (3.5.36)
14. Meditation in Anger: The Lord fulfilled their desires to always remember H i m ,
but becoming demons they will have to meditate in anger thus they will always
remain firmly united with the Lord despite their separation. (3.16.26)
DEVAHÜTI
15. She allows L o r d Kapila to leave: Knowing the truth about her son, Devahüti did
not mind H i m leaving, knowing that she would be protected by His will. (3.33.12
V)
Thematic Compilations
16. Follows instructions i n H i s absence: She remained alone at home and practiced
bhakti-yoga as instructed by her son. (3.33.13)
17. Intense feelings of separation i n vatsalya: First losing her husband and then her
son, she was aggrieved like a cow that has lost its calf. "Please bring my son
home so that I may live. Otherwise, I shall die." (3.33.21 P)
18. Detachment: Due to her meditation on the Lord in separation, she lost all
attachment for her beautiful home. (3.33.22)
19. Intense meditation: Through her intense meditation on the Lord's form, as
taught by her son, she gradually attained Bhagaván realization and returned to
the planet of Lord Kapila in the spiritual world. (3.33.30 P)

8 . i D E A L HOUSEHOLD LIFE
The grhastha-ásrama,   or householder life, is considered the support for the entire
varnasrama system, considering that the other three orders -              brahmacdrl,
vdnaprastha and sannydsi - are dependent on them for personal maintenance. The
Third Canto portrays valuahle exemplary dealings and lessons for an ideal
householder based on the characters of Sváyambhuva Manu, Kardama Muni and
Devahüti, as well as Kasyapa Muni and Diti. The grhastha-dsrama is a life of sacrifice
and prime responsibilities. The following article presents points given in the
Bhdgavatam as well as from Srila Prabhupada's purports that highiight the principles
of the ideal grhastha-dsrama.
DEFINITIONS
1.   There are two kinds of householders - grhamedhi and grhastha. The objective of
the grhamedhi is sense gratification, and the objective of the grhastha is self-
realization. (3.32.1 P)
2.   Grhastha refers to one who lives with family, wife and children, hut whose real
purpose of living is to develop Krsna consciousness. (3.30.8 P)
1.   Household life should be conducted in such a way that one not go against the
principles of dharma, artha and kdma, but elevate oneself in Krsna consciousness.
(3.22.33 P)
Example: Manu enjoyed life according to dharma (3.22.33)
2.   T o lead a life of piety, follow the religious rules and regulations, marry and live
peacefuUy for elevation to the higher status of spiritual realization. (3.21.16 P)
MWmMMMMMWmMMMMWM
Thematic Compilations
3.   To give people in generál the best opportunity to utilize their humán life in the
midst of matériái enjoyment, by reguiating their habits of sense enjoyment.
(3.22.34 P)
4.   Generating good progeny under the blessings of advanced devotees. (3.21.6)
)UTiES O F AN IDEAL H O U S E H O L D E R •
1.   Enjoying worldly life without conflicting religion. (3.22.33)
2. To not spend time in vain but to contemplate, hear and chant about the pastimes
of the Lord. (3.22.35)
3.   The fame of a householder increases by getting their offspring married to a
suitable match. (3.24.15)
4. They should follow the purificatory process of garbhddhána-samskara      to produce
good progeny. (3.20.28 P)
5.   When the husband and wife aim at spiritual advancement by mutual cooperation
there will be no disruption in their relationship when sense gratification stops.
(3.14.19 P)
6. The grhastha is responsible for producing first-quality brahmacdris,    vdnaprasthas
and sannydsis. (3.14.20 P)
S E L E C T I N G T H E RiGHT S P O U S L
1.   One should select a wife of like disposition and not be enamored by so-called
heauty or other attractive features for sense gratification. (3.21.15 P)
2.   Consult with astrology. (3.21.15 P)
3.   Man and woman should be united based on character and taste, not on sex life.
(3.24.15 P)
4. A woman seeks a like-minded husband in terms of age, character and good
qualities. (3.21.27, 3.22.9)
5.   Only by God's grace can one get a nice suitable spouse. (3.21.28 P)
6.   Generally, men should be of a higher social order compared to wife, nevertheless
by special permission, the opposite is also possible. (3.21.28 P)
7. The boy's training is brahmacarya, and the girl's training is chastity. A faithful
wife and spiritually trained brahmacdrl are a good combination for advancement
of the humán mission. (3.14.17 P)

SoME Q U A L I T I E S O F A G O O D W I F E
1.   The wife is accepted as the better half of a man's body because she is supposed to
be responsible for discharging half of the husband's duties. (3.14.19 P)
2. The wife is dependent on the husband, and if the husband is a 'Vaisnava, then
naturally she shares the devotional service of the husband because she renders
him service. (3.23.1 P)
3. A wife serves her husband with intimacy and great respect, with control of the
senses, with love and with sweet words, understanding the husband's desires.
(3.23.2)
4.   A faithful and chaste wife helps her husband in every respect, especially when the
husband is engaged in Krsna consciousness. (3.23.6 P)
SoME Q U A L I T I E S O F A G O O D H U S B A N D       '
1.   The husband should be enlightened and become the spiritual master of the wife.
(3.24.5 P)
2.   The husband has to renounce household life at the proper time. (3.23.3)
3. A n ideal husband is not in the sense of riches or sense gratification, but is in the
sense of being a devotee {Example: Lord Siva) (3.23.1 P)
4.   It is the responsibility of the husband to give his wife fearlessness. (3.23.51 P)
5.   He must also provide all comforts to the wife, but not become materially
attacbed. (3.33.19 P)

The baseline of devotional practice is obedience - to the Lord, to His devotees and to
the process of devotional service. Obedience is foUowed not just by accepting an
order, but also in executing it and the overall mood that one carries throughout.
Although seemingly a tough pill to swallow for most, practicing obedience is made
easier by the exemplary characters of devotees found in the Bhágavatam as well as
the enlightening instruction given by Srila Prabhupáda in his purports. This
compilation attempts to present relevant case studies, principles and lessons of
obedience presented in the third canto.
C A S E STUDIES
1 . Kardama Muni to Brahmá
Kardama Muni was instructed to populate the world by his father, lord Brahmá. I n
lieu of that command, he practiced penance on the bank of the River Sarasvati for a
period of 10,000 years (3.21.6) and satisfied Lord Visnu. After he begets ten children,
lord Brahmá appears and expresses his heart to Kardama, saying "Since you have
completely accepted my instructions without duplicity, showing them proper respect,
you have worshiped me properly, and thereby honored me." (3.24.12)
2. Sváyambhuva Manu to Brahmá
The father of mankind, Manu prayed to Brahmá, "Please order us how we may be
able to rendet service unto y o u . " (3.13.7) "Please give us your direction for the
execution of duty within our working capacity so that we can follow it for fame in
this life and progress in the next." (3.13.8)
Lord Brahmá replied, " M y dear son, O lord of the world, I am very pleased with you,
and I desire all blessings for both you and your wife. Y o u have without reservation
surrendered yourself unto me with your heart for my instructions (3.13.9). Manu
replies, " O all-powerful lord, O killer of all sins, I shall abide by your order."
(3.13.14)
3. Rúdra to Brahmá
When Rúdra was generated, he began creating all kinds of destructive beings that
devastated creation. Brahmá warned him of this and thus asked that he rétire and
perform penance, until the time for destruction arrived. (3.12.17-19) Thus Rúdra
circumambulated his father, Brahmá, addressing him with words of assent {bádham
iti) and entered the forest to perform austere penances. (3.12.20)
4 . Uddhava to Lord Krsna
While the rest of the Yádavas ended their earthly pastimes, Uddhava alone remained.
Lord Krsna considering Uddhava no way inferior than Himself, wanted Uddhava to
remain in this world after His disappearance and disseminate the knowiedge of the
Supreme by meeting with the sages at Badarikásrama (3.4.28-32). Although Uddhava
was anxious about the imminent separation form the Lord, to mitigate his separation,
he proceeded to Badarikásrama for association (3.4.21). Thus, Uddhava, as a pure
devotee of the Lord, carried out the order of Krsna more faithfuUy than going to
abode of the Lord. (3.4.28 P)
instruction
6.   Srila Visvanátha Cakravarti Tháküra has stated that the order of the spiritual
master is the life and soul of the disciples. As a man cannot separate his life from
his body, a disciple cannot separate the order of the spiritual master from his life.
(3.24.13 P)
7. The order of the father or spiritual master must be carried out without hesitation
or argument but with an immediate yes. That is real service to the father or
spiritual master. (3.24.13 P)
8.   This combination, the instruction of the spiritual master and the faithful
execution of the instruction by the disciple, makes the entire process perfect.
(3.22.7 P)
9. Devotional service entails being initiated by a hona fide spiritual master and
following his instruction in regard to hearing about the Lord. (3.5.42 P)
Execution
10. Execution of the Lord's service is the main occupation of the pure devotee (3.4.21
P)
11. As long as one is engaged in the execution of the order of the Lord, there is no
factual separation from H i m . (3.4.21 P)
12. One should be very careful to receive the message from the spiritual master
through the ears and execute it faithfully. That will make one's life successful.
(3.22.7 P)
Etiquette
13. One should he very careful about impertinently surpassing a superior, because by
so doing one loses his duration of life, his opulence, fame and piety and the
blessings of all the world. (3.4.26 P)
14. One should never pass over the honor due to an elderly spiritual master in the
interests of one's own personal gain and fame. Impertinence on the part of the
pseudo spiritual master is very risky to progressive spiritual realization (3.4.26 P)
Attitűdé
15. One should obey the command of his father or spiritual master with due
deference, saying, "Yes, sir (bádham i t i ) " (3.24.23)
16. There should be no instance in which the disciple or son says, "This is not
correct. I cannot carry it out." When he says that, he is fallen. (3.24.13 P).
17. Whether one is liberated or not liberated, one should execute the instruction of
the spiritual master with great faith. (3.24.5 P)
18. A person entrusted with responsibility should always be aware of his incapability
and must always look for the mercy of the Lord for the successful execution of
his duty. (3.9.29 P)

Beauty of the Lord in H i s different forms is described throughout the third Canto.
Civen below are somé of those descriptions in brief. More details of the Lord's form
may be found in the originál text.
The ornament of all ornaments. [3.2.12]
Demigods exclaimed - Ultimate dexterous creation of Brahma. [3.2.13]
Copís would follow H i m with their eyes, and being stunned, they could not finish
their household duties. [3.2.14]
Warriors of Kuruksetra, while seeing the lotuslike face of Krsna, so pleasing to
the eyes, achieved the abode of the Lord. [3.2.20]
L O R D KRSISA'S D A R S A N A T O U D D H A V A '
Four hands with different symbols and yellow silk garments. [3.4.7]
Sitting, resting against a young banyán tree, with His right lotus foot on His left
thigh. [3.4.8]
DARSANA O F GARBHODAKASÁYÍ V I S N U T O L O R D BRAHMA
Lying alone on the gigantic white body of Sesa-nága. The jewels on the hoods of
Sesanága illuminated all the directions. [3.8.23]
Luster of His transcendental body mocked the beauty of coral mountain. [3.8.24]
Occupying the planetary systems His body was self-illuminated, well-ornamented
and unlimited in iength and breadth. [3.8.25]
The Lord (Krsna) showed His lotus feet by raising them. His splendorous
moonlike toenails appeared like the petals of a flower. [3.8.26]
His smile, nose, eyebrows, rays from lips and earrings are all beautiful. [3.8.27]
His form is paralleled with the beauty of a sandalwood tree and a great
mountain. [3.8.29-30]

DARSANA O F LORD VARÁHA
Came as a small boar out of Brahmá's nostril, about thumb size and instantly
turnéd into a wonderful manifestation as gigantic as an elephant. [3.13.18-19]
Flying in the sky with tail slashing and hard hairs quivering with a luminous

Killing the démon. He appeared just as an elephant becomes reddish from digging

tusks. He assumed a bluish complexion like that of a tamála tree. [3.13.33]
He is the form of the personified Vedas, and all H i s bodily limbs are compared
with different parts of a sacrifice. [3.13.34-38]
D A R S A N A O F L O R D V I S N U T O KUMÁRAS
Accompanied by associates bearing all paraphernalia. He personally spreads the
beauty and good fortune of the spiritual world. [3.15.38-41]
Resting one of His hands on the shoulder of Caruda, and twirled a lotus with
another hand. [3.15.40]
Lord's beauty, being many times magnified by the intelligence of His devotees,
defeated the pride of Laksmi. [3.15.42]
His face is like the inside of a blue lotus, His smile is like jasmine flower and His
lotus feet resemble rubies. [3.15.44]

Adorned with a crown and earrings, holding saiikha, cakra, gadá and a white lily,
His smiling glancé captivates devotees. [3.21.10]
Lndowed with Srivatsa and Kaustubha, He stood in the air with His lotus feet
placed on the shoulders of Caruda. [3.21.11]
Description of the overall beauty (3.28.13-20) as well as limb-by-limb (3.28.21-33)
description of the four handed form of Lord Visnu.

Biography
1.    Born: of Vyásadeva (Vicitravirya's brother) and a maidservant. (3.5.19-20)
2.    Formerly: Yamarája, but was cursed by Mándavya Muni to be born as a südra. (3.5.20-
21)
3.    Name: Indicates an expert advisor, politically renowned and instructions exactly to the
point. (3.1.10)
4.    He is a pure devotee of the Lord. (3.1.4)
Activities
5.    Pilgrimage: After Duryodhana evicted him, Vidura went for a pilgrimage and completely
cleansed himself of all contaminations {virajds tirtha-sevayd). (3.1.17-24, 3.20.4)
6.    Learning: As suggested by Uddhava, he approached Maitreya for receiving
transcendental knowiedge. (3.4.26, 3.4.36)
Qualities
7.    Bold: Instructed Dhrtarástra to return the kingdom to the Pándavas. (3.1.11-13)
8.    Inquiries: are meaningful, purposeful and Krsna conscious. (3.1.4, 3.1.45, 3.14.5)
9.    Humble: Accepted spiritual knowiedge from a younger person, Uddhava (3.4.25)
10.   Optimist: He used the insult of Duryodhana for cultivating higher realization. (3.1.2 P,
3.1.16)
11.   Desire: Great appetite for hearing transcendental activities of Krsna. (3.14.1)
Interactions
12. Uddhava: Affectionately embraces and eagerly asks of the Yadu's welfare. (3.1.25)
13. Krsna: They were great friends. Krsna would sometimes place His feet on his lap.
(3.13.5)

UDDHAVA
Biography
1.   Nitya-siddha: At 5 years old, he would be so absorbed in his service to Krsna that when
called for breakfast by his mother, he would not come. (3.2.2-3)
2.   Vasú: He expanded as one of the eight Vasus (demigods) and desired to become one of
the Lord's associates. (3.4.11)
3.   Disciple: Great student of Brhaspati's (3.1.25)
Qualities
4.   Respectful: He bowed down to when he met Vidura (3.1.25).
5.   Mannered: Being much younger than Vidura, he sent him to Maitreya Rsi out of
etiquette. (3.4.26)
Activities
6.   Counsel: He would be called by Krsna for consultation on several topics (3.4.17)
7.   Companion: He was a constant companion of Lord Krsna (3.1.25)
Interactions with Lord Krsna
8. Lord Krsna's cousin (3.1.25 P)
9. Showed immense feelings of separation from Krsna. (3.2.1, 3.2.3-6)
10. Lord Krsna instructed him of His transcendental situation (paramdm sthitim) which is
considered deeper than the Catuh-sloki Bhdgavatam. (3.4.18-19 P)
11. Lord Krsna instructed Uddhava to go to Badarikásrama. He worshiped Nara and
Náráyana out of deep affiiction due to separation (3.4.21-22).
12. Sukadeva explained Lord Krsna's thoughts: "Uddhava is the foremost of My devotees,
beyond the influence of modes and not even inferior to Me. I will entrust Him as My
representative to disseminate knowiedge of Me on earth" (3.4.30-31)
MAITREYA RSI
Biography
1.   Fatber: Son of Kusáru, tbus also known as Kausárava. (3.5.15, 3.19.33)
2.   Guru: hecame the spiritual master of Vidura during his pilgrimage. (1.19.9-10 P)
3.   Mahá-bhágavata: disciple of Vyásadeva's father. Párására Muni. Thus Vyásadeva and
he were good childhood friends. (3.4.9, 3.25.4)
4.   Jnána-bhakta: Although great, he was considered a mixed devotee at the time when he
met Krsna and Uddhava. (3.4.10 P)
5.   Heard Bhágavatam: from Párására, on being advised by Pulastya. (3.8.9)

TA
. KRSNA'S NAMES
The Srimad-Bhagavatam purifies its sincere readers by narrations of krsna-kathá and also
tbrougb the beautiful names of Krsna that are mentioned throughout its pages. These boly
names are intended to glorify the Lord, but they simultaneously purify tbe reader by
immersing him in the beauty of Krsna's qualities and activities. As this Third Canto promises,
simply by reciting tbe Lord's holy names, one is guaranteed to make spiritual advancement
(3.33.6), overcome innumerable sufferings (3.7.14) and purify himself, no matter in what
fallen condition he is. (3.16.6).
The following is a list of the Lord's names taken from the verses of the third canto
categorized into three sections.
1.   Names that highiight Lord's personal attributes.
1.   Names that relate the Lord with matériái nature
2. Qualifiers that bring out the character of the Lord.
It should be noted that somé names will fali into more than one of tbese three categories.
Although somé of tbese names appear repeatedly, we took only one instance of the name.

J^^SAINTLY QUALITIES^J
The Supreme Personality of Godhead has unlimited good and transcendental qualities and His
sincere devotees reflect these qualities in their behavior and personality. The study of Srimad-
Bhágavatam allows us to bask in the light of saintly qualities, reflecting upon them and
ultimately trying to apply them in our lives. Saintly persons bridge our relationship with the
Lord and thus by following their footsteps, we come closer to tbe Lord. The following is a lest
of saintly qualities we could learn from various devotees described in Canto 3.

ímMmMmm                                                      4. Analogies
Conveying a message becomes simple and easy witb tbe usage of analogies. We find
tbis very commonly in our scriptures and even day-to-day life. Several speakers in tbe
Bbágavatam like Sukadeva Gosvámi and Maitreya rsi used fitting analogies to present
deep pbilosopbical points and Srila Prabhupáda furtber elaborates tbe message of tbe
Bhágavatam witb contemporary analogies tbe modern man can relate to. Following is
a üst analogies from tbe verses and purports of Canto 3, duly categorized.
Note: Subjects/objects/things marked with same color are compared with each other
within a particular analogy. Example: Fire - Lord, friction - krpa-sakti
The Lord's appearance and disappearance:
1.   Just as fire is inberent in all elements, so tbe Lord is eternally present in tbe Holy
Dbáma. Just as fire is made visible by friction between jewels or stones, similarly
tbe Lord becomes visible by His krpa-sakti when His devotees are afflicted by tbe
demons. (3.2.15)
2.   Lord Krsna's appearance and disappearance are exactly like the rising and setting
of tbe sun. He appears and disappears in innumerable universes continuously. (3.2.7
V)
3.   At sunset tbe snakes become powerful, tbieves are encouraged, ghosts become
active, tbe lotus becomes disfigured and tbe cakraváki laments, so witb tbe
disappearance of Lord Krsna, tbe atheists feel enlivened, and the devotees become
sorry. (3.2.7 P)
Proper and mistaken knowiedge about the Lord's position:
4.   Just as one candle is used to light otber candles and altbougb all of them blaze witb
tbe same intensity, tbe initial one remains tbe originál. Similarly, altbougb there is no
difference between the various expansions of tbe Lord and Himself, tbe Lord
remains tbe Originál Personality of Godhead. (3.21.32 P)
5.  Just as fire is different from sparks, wood and smoke, Paramátmá is different from
the jiva, pradhána and body, senses and mind as He is the witness. Just as foolish
incorrectly consider sparks, wood and smoke to be tbe fire, less intelligent men
mistake tbe pradhána, the jiva, and bis body to be tbe svarüpa of tbe Lord.
(3.28.41 V)
6. What appears to be a cloud from a distance, and a big bili from closer proximity,
appears as a place of great variety when one is actually at tbe bili. One will similarly
be able to appreciate tbe Absolute Truth according to one's proximity to Him.
(3.32.33 P)
7. Tbe Supreme Lord, wbo is tbe originál fire, is never overpowered by máyá, but
jivas, wbo are sparks of the fire, are subjected to máyá's influence. (3.7.9 P)
8. Just as tbe stomach is tbe source of energy of all the limbs of the body, God is tbe
originál source of all energy manifested in the matériái and spiritual worlds (3.18.5
P)
9. Tbe sun is only one, yet tbe experience of every man at noon is that tbe sun is
directly on top of bis bead alone. Similarly, tbe one Supersoul oversees every
individual, but He appears to individually oversee each individual soul. (3.22.4 P)
10. Just as an authorized post-box of tbe postai system, performs tbe function of
carrying letters, tbe arcá-mürti of tbe Lord delivers the same unlimited potency
of Him wbicb can be experienced when He is personally present. (3.1.18 P)
11. Tbe members of tbe Yadu dynasty considered Krsna similar to themselves just as
tbe fisb, not understanding tbe moon's position, could mistakenly identify its
reflection in tbe ocean to be similar to itself. (3.2.8)
12. Just as a frog in a well cannot measure tbe lengtb and breadth of tbc Pacific Ocean,
a mundane Pb.D. person cannot perceive tbe transcendental nature of tbe Supreme
witb bis limited senses. (3.6.10 P)
Lord's ínteraction with matéria! nature:
13. Just as tbe painter's presence is required to produce a paiut of a specific color by
mixing different colours, tbe Lord's glauce is last finisbing touch at every stage in
the process of creation. (3.5.36 P)
14. A magician may appear as a cow by bis magicai talent, yet tbe cow does not
represent bis actual personality or nature. Similarly, tbc Lord appears as the
universal form by His máyá-sakti, altbougb tbe universal form is certainly not tbe
Supreme Lord. (3.7.2 P)
4. Analogies
15. Just as a King may visit the prison out of his own good will, but is never subject to
its restrictions, similarly, tbe Lord descends on tbe eartb by His own internál
potency, and is never subject to contamination by tbe modes of matériái nature.
(3.7.2 P)
16. Just as tbe clouds, darkness, and snow, altbougb created by sun, bave no power to
influence tbe sun, similarly, máyá, created by the Lord, has no power to cover Him.
(3.7.9 P)
17. Just as a spider creates, maintains, and unwinds its cobwebs by its own energy, the
Lord independently, by His own energy, creates, maintains, and destroys tbe
matériái world. (3.21.19)
18. As fire distributes its light and beat from one place, tbe Supreme Personality of
Godhead distributes His different energies all over His creation. (3.28.41 P, 3.7.3 P)
Lord's relationship w i t h the j i v a :
19. The living entity and tbe Lord as Supersoul are like two birds seated on a tree of
tbe body. Tbe individual soul is eating tbe fruit of tbe tree, but tbe Supersoul, is
witnessing tbe activities of tbe otber bird, just as a citizen of state is witnessed by
state autbority. (3.7.6 P)
20. Just as the Ganges nourisbes all the trees situated at its banks, the Lord nourisbes
every living entity. However, just as each tree produces different fruits due to its
own differing qualities, every living entity exbibits different traits according to bis
past karma. (3.5.38 P)
21. The Lord or tbe spiritual master is like an axe that cut tbe tree of matériái
existence for tbe disciple. (3.25.11)
22. Tbe Lord is like the sun. He illuminates tbe darkness of tbe conditional life of tbe
living entities. (3.29.5)
23. Just as an umbrella protects from beat of the sun, the Lord's lotus feet protects tbe
surrendered devotees from tbe sun of repeated birth and death. (3.21.17 P)
Lord's pastimes:
24. Just as tbe sun rises from the ocean and then enlivens tbe lotus flower in tbe
morning, tbe Lord first appeared from the womb of Devaki, and gradually
satisfied tbe inbabitants of tbe places surrounding Matburá. (3.2.15 P)
25. Tbe unborn Lord's birtb, fleeing to Vraja in fear of Kaihsa, and establisbing
Dváraká in fear of Jarásandba are enbancing His supreme glories. It is just like a
fatber's playful defeat by bis little son - tbe purpose is to give pleasure to tbe son,
and nothing more. (3.2.16, P)
26. Just as a cbild breaks so many dolls witbout any real endeavour, the Lord can
similarly kill millions of demons. (3.19.32 P)

27. Just like an actor performs play on a stage, the Lord's incarnations imitate the
activities of the matériái world. (3.9.15)
28. Just as pure milk is poisoned by a serpent's touch, supremely pure pastimes of tbe
Lord bave no effect, when spoken by nondevotees and it also becomes poisonous.
(3.19.33 P)
29. Just as a king is always associated witb bis secretaries, commanders, servants, etc,
the Lord is always surrounded by bis associates. (3.25.38 P)
30. Tbe narrations of tbe spotless pastimes of the Lord are exactly like batbing in tbe
water of the Ganges. They can free one from all sinful reacrions. (3.20.5)
31. A cobra is very fierce before ordinary persons, but before an encbanter be is a
playtbing. Similarly, a démon may be very powerful in bis domain, but before tbc
Lord be is insignificant. (3.18.13 P)

Qualities and Nature of Bhakti:
32. Just as tbe water of the Ganges flows naturally, uninterrupted, down towards the            i
ocean, similarly tbrougb unadulterated, uninterrupted devotional service one's
mind is at once attracted to bearing tbe transcendental name and qualities of the
Lord. (3.29.12)
33. Just as tbe eternal function of tbe fire is warmtb, tbe real dharma or constitutional
engagement of the soul, is engagement in tbe service of tbe Lord. (3.25.11 P)
34. J U S T as after awakening a person forgets things seen in a dream, similarly the jiva
who is Krsna Conscious forgers all bis cbanges of body. (3.33.26 P)
35. Just as the milk is perceived partially as white, palatable, cold, fragrant, and its
nomenclature as milk tbrougb eye, tongue, skin, nose and ears respectively, but
only the mind, tbe king of all senses, can perceive it witb all tbe qualities. Similarly,
tbe Lord is perceived partially as Brahman or Paramátmá tbrougb various
processes seeking Him, but only by bhakti alone He is seen face-to-face as tbe
Supreme Person. (3.32.33 V)
36. Brahraánanda is like a fig in comparison to devotional service wbicb awards an
ocean of bliss. (3.5.13, P)
37. Just as an ocean is unagitated by the constant flow of many rivers into it, similarly
a sober minded person full in Krsna consciousness is uninfluenced by multifarious
matériái desires. (3.24.44 P)
38. Just as in clear, unagitated water one can see everything clearly, similarly in pure
consciousness one can see tbe reflection of Lord and one's own existence. (3.26.22 P)
'^MMJ^MMJ^^&:                                                4. Analogies
39. Just as the sun reflected on water appears to be on tbe water but is many millions
of miles away from the water, similarly a person engaged in tbe bhakti-yoga
process is unaffected by matériái nature. (3.27.1 P)
Process of bhakti:
40. Tbe process of bhakti-yoga is tbe main river flowing down towards tbe sea of tbe
Absolute Truth, and all other processes like karma-yoga, jnána-yoga and astánga-
yoga are just like its tributaries. (3.32.37 P)
41. Just as tbe fire of digestion digests all that we eat, similarly the process of bhakti
dissolves tbe subtle body of tbe jiva witbout separate effort. (3.25.33 V)
42. Just as a man cannot separate bis life from bis body, a disciple cannot separate tbe
order of tbe spiritual master from bis life. (3.24.13 P)
43. Just as the sky can never be filled up, similarly tbe ears cannot become exbausted by
receiving more and more vibrations. (3.15.49 P)
44. Just as a student in a law class is considered to be already graduated from generál
education, similarly a person engaged in tbe chanting of tbe boly names of tbe Lord
has already passed all lower stages. (3.33.7 P)
45. Just as tbe chariot of air carries an aroma from its source and immediately catcbes
tbe sense of smell, similarly, the chariot of bhakti allows one to catcb tbe
transcendental existence of the Supreme Soul. (3.29.20)
46. Just as during yajha pouring oblations into asbes is a waste of time and resources,
similarly tbe Deity worship tainted witb disrespect of tbe Paramátmá by unkind
treatment to another jiva is of no use, as it's in ignorance. (3.29.22 P)
47. Just as a child stops all bis mischief upon receiving a better engagement, the senses
come under one's control upon giving them a bigber engagement. (3.7.13 P)
48. Just as bomeless souls following tbe path of birds making their way back to their
nest on a tree, find shelter under tbe base of tbe same tree, similarly sages
enlightened by tbe birds of Vedic knowiedge, emanating from tbe nest of tbe Lord's
lotus moutb, find shelter at tbe Lord's lotus feet. (3.5.41 V)
Results of bhakti:
49. Just as the miseries perceived in a dream are vanquisbed in deep sleep, similarly tbe
dream-like miseries of tbe matériái world are vanquisbed by complete absorption
in the Lord, Wbo attracts each of tbe senses of His devotees. (3.7.13 V)
50. Just as tbe biting of tbe snake witbout poisonous teeth is not fatál, similarly
accumulation of tbe wealtb for tbe Lord has no poisonous effect. (3.9.6)
